# List 20

## Unit 1

<table>
<thead><tr>
<th>单词</th>
<th>音标</th>
<th>常用</th>
<th>口语</th>
<th>词性</th>
<th>中文释义</th>
<th>常用短语</th>
</tr></thead>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>prowess</b></td><td>/ˈpraʊəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>高超技艺；英勇</td><td>athletic prowess<br>过人的运动本领</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His athletic prowess earned him a scholarship at a famous university.  他出色的运动才能为他赢得了一所名校的奖学金。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 bravery</td><td>/ˈbreɪvəri/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>勇敢，英勇</td><td>an act of bravery<br>勇敢之举</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She was given a medal for bravery during the flood.  她因在洪水中的勇敢而获得奖章。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 courageousness</del></td><td>/kəˈreɪdʒəsnəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>勇敢，胆量</td><td>quiet courageousness<br>沉静的勇气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her courageousness during the rescue was mentioned by every witness.  每个目击者都提到她在救援中的勇敢。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 daring</td><td>/ˈderɪŋ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词、形容词</td><td>勇敢，大胆</td><td>an act of daring<br>大胆之举</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Climbing that face alone took real daring and long preparation.  独自攀那面岩壁需要真正的胆量和长期准备。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 gallantry</td><td>/ˈɡæləntri/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>英勇；殷勤</td><td>an award for gallantry<br>英勇奖章</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He received a medal for gallantry after pulling three men out.  他救出三个人后获得了英勇奖章。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 guts</td><td>/ɡʌts/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>勇气，胆量；内脏</td><td>have the guts<br>有胆量</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She had the guts to quit and start her own company.  她有胆量辞职创办自己的公司。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 intrepidity</td><td>/ˌɪntrəˈpɪdəti/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>勇敢，无畏</td><td>show great intrepidity<br>表现出极大勇气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The pilot's intrepidity saved everyone aboard that night.  那晚飞行员的无畏救了机上所有人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 timid</td><td>/ˈtɪmɪd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>胆怯的，羞怯的</td><td>a timid smile<br>怯生生的微笑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She gave a timid smile and slipped into the back row.  她怯生生地一笑，溜进后排座位。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 cowardice</td><td>/ˈkaʊərdɪs/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>怯懦，胆小</td><td>an act of cowardice<br>怯懦之举</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Refusing to speak up for a friend struck him later as cowardice.  没有为朋友说话，事后他觉得那是怯懦。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 cravenness</del></td><td>/ˈkreɪvənnəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>极度怯懦，胆小</td><td>sheer cravenness<br>十足的懦弱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His cravenness under fire shocked the men who once admired him.  他在炮火下的懦弱令曾敬佩他的人震惊。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 dastardliness</del></td><td>/ˈdæstərdlinəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>卑怯，怯懦无耻</td><td>the dastardliness of the attack<br>这次袭击的卑劣</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The dastardliness of the ambush disgusted even their own allies.  这次伏击的卑劣连他们自己的盟友都感到恶心。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 poltroonery</del></td><td>/pɑlˈtrunəri/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>怯懦，胆小如鼠</td><td>unforgivable poltroonery<br>不可原谅的怯懦</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The general denounced such poltroonery before the whole assembled regiment.  将军在全团面前谴责这种怯懦。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>prowl</b></td><td>/ˈpraʊl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>巡游以猎取食物；徘徊</td><td>prowl the dark streets<br>在暗街徘徊猎食</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Hungry foxes prowl the dark streets long after the market closes.  市场关门很久之后，饥饿的狐狸仍在暗街徘徊觅食。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>prude</b></td><td>/pruːd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>过分正经的人</td><td>a prim old prude<br>一本正经的老古板</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her neighbors dismissed her as a prim old prude who disliked laughter.  邻居们把她看作一个不喜欢笑声的一本正经的老古板。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 bluenose</td><td>/ˈbluːˌnoʊz/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>过分拘谨的人，道德家</td><td>a small-town bluenose<br>小镇上的老古板</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Some bluenose on the council wants the mural painted over.  议会里某个老古板想把这幅壁画涂掉。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 moralist</td><td>/ˈmɔːrəlɪst/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>道德家，爱说教者</td><td>a stern moralist<br>严厉的道德家</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The novel's narrator is a moralist who judges everyone harshly.  小说的叙述者是个对人人都苛评的道德家。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 puritan</td><td>/ˈpjʊrətən/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>生活严谨的人；清教徒</td><td>a puritan about money<br>对花钱极严谨的人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 My aunt is a puritan about money and refuses all credit.  我姑姑在金钱上极为严谨，拒绝一切信贷。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 immoralist</del></td><td>/ɪˈmɔrəlɪst/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>不道德的人，反道德者</td><td>a self-declared immoralist<br>自称的反道德者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The poet posed as an immoralist to shock respectable readers.  这位诗人装作反道德者，以震惊体面的读者。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>prudent</b></td><td>/ˈpruːdnt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>明智的；小心谨慎的，审慎的</td><td>a prudent investment<br>一笔审慎的投资</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It would be prudent to save part of every paycheck.  每份工资都存下一部分才是明智之举。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 advisable</td><td>/ədˈvaɪzəbəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>明智的，可取的</td><td>it seems advisable<br>这似乎是明智的</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It is advisable to book your tickets several weeks early.  提前几周订票是明智的做法。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 desirable</td><td>/dɪˈzaɪrəbəl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>可取的，明智合宜的</td><td>a desirable compromise<br>可取的折中方案</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Delaying the vote seemed the most desirable course available.  推迟投票似乎是最可取的做法。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 judicious</td><td>/dʒuˈdɪʃəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>明智的，慎重的</td><td>a judicious choice<br>明智的选择</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A judicious use of savings carried the family through two difficult years.  明智地使用积蓄让这家人度过了两个艰难的年头。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 politic</td><td>/ˈpɑːlətɪk/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>合时宜的，明智的，高情商的</td><td>a politic silence<br>明智的沉默</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It would not be politic to mention his failure at the dinner.  在晚宴上提他的失败并不明智。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 tactical</td><td>/ˈtæktɪkəl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>策略上明智的；战术的</td><td>a tactical retreat<br>策略性的撤退</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Staying silent was a tactical choice, not weakness.  保持沉默是策略上的选择，而非软弱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 intelligent</td><td>/ɪnˈtelədʒənt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>明智的，聪明的</td><td>an intelligent decision<br>明智的决定</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Selling the old plant was an intelligent decision.  卖掉旧厂是个明智的决定。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 foolish</td><td>/ˈfuːlɪʃ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>愚蠢的，傻的</td><td>a foolish mistake<br>愚蠢的错误</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It was foolish of him to ignore the warning signs.  他忽视警示标志实在愚蠢。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>prudish</b></td><td>/ˈpruːdɪʃ/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>过分守礼的</td><td>a prudish attitude<br>过分守礼的态度</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His prudish attitude toward modern art amused the younger students.  他对现代艺术过分守礼的态度让年轻学生觉得好笑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 nice-nelly</td><td>/ˈnaɪs ˈnɛli/</td><td>★★</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>假正经的（人）</td><td>nice-nelly language<br>假正经的措辞</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The editor replaced every blunt word with nice-nelly substitutes.  编辑把每个直白的词都换成假正经的说法。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 prim</td><td>/ˈprɪm/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>拘谨的，一本正经的</td><td>prim and proper<br>一本正经</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She sat very prim on the edge of the borrowed chair.  她一本正经地坐在借来的椅子边上。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 puritanical</td><td>/ˌpjʊrəˈtænɪkəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>极严谨的，清教徒式的</td><td>a puritanical upbringing<br>极严谨的家教</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His puritanical upbringing left him uneasy about any luxury.  极严谨的家教让他对任何奢侈都不自在。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>prune</b></td><td>/pruːn/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>修剪；修整</td><td>prune the rose bushes<br>修剪玫瑰丛</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 We should prune the rose bushes before the first frost arrives.  我们应该在初霜到来之前修剪玫瑰丛。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 shave</td><td>/ˈʃeɪv/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>刮，剃；削去</td><td>shave a thin layer<br>削掉薄薄一层</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The carpenter shaved a thin layer off the sticking door.  木匠把卡住的门削掉薄薄一层。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 shear</td><td>/ˈʃɪr/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>动词</td><td>剪（羊毛）；修剪</td><td>shear the sheep<br>给羊剪毛</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They shear the whole flock over two long spring weekends.  他们用春天两个长周末给整群羊剪毛。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 snip</td><td>/ˈsnɪp/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>剪，剪断</td><td>snip off the ends<br>剪掉末端</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She snipped off the dead ends before putting the flowers in water.  她把枯掉的末端剪掉再把花插进水里。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 trim</td><td>/ˈtrɪm/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>修剪；削减</td><td>trim the hedge<br>修剪树篱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He trims the hedge every June before the neighbors complain.  他每年六月在邻居抱怨前修剪树篱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>pry</b></td><td>/praɪ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>刺探，打听</td><td>pry into his private life<br>打探他的私生活</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Reporters continued to pry into his private life for many weeks.  记者们连续数周不断打探他的私生活。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 interlope</del></td><td>/ˌɪntərˈloʊp/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>闯入，多管闲事</td><td>interlope in a private talk<br>闯入私下的谈话</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He interloped in the negotiation without any invitation at all.  他毫无邀请就插进了谈判。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 intermeddle</del></td><td>/ˌɪntərˈmɛdəl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>干涉，插手</td><td>intermeddle in local politics<br>插手地方政治</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Foreign advisers intermeddled in the country's internal affairs.  外国顾问插手了该国内政。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 intrude</td><td>/ɪnˈtruːd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>侵入，打扰</td><td>intrude on her privacy<br>侵犯她的隐私</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 I hope I am not intruding on your quiet evening.  希望我没有打扰你安静的夜晚。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 nose</td><td>/ˈnoʊz/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>打探，探听；嗅</td><td>nose into private matters<br>打探私事</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Reporters nosed around the office for two days looking for leaks.  记者在办公室四处打探了两天，想找到泄密者。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 obtrude</del></td><td>/əbˈtruːd/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>动词</td><td>强加，硬挤入；侵扰</td><td>obtrude one's opinions<br>强行发表意见</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He obtruded his advice on everyone at the table.  他向桌上每个人强行灌输自己的建议。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 snoop</td><td>/ˈsnuːp/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>窥探，探听</td><td>snoop around the office<br>在办公室四处窥探</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Someone had been snooping around my desk all week.  整整一周都有人在我桌子附近窥探。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>pseudonym</b></td><td>/ˈsuːdənɪm/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>假名，笔名</td><td>write under a pseudonym<br>用笔名写作</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She published her early detective novels under a masculine pseudonym.  她早期的侦探小说是用一个男性笔名发表的。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 alias</td><td>/ˈeɪliəs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>别名，化名</td><td>travel under an alias<br>用化名旅行</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The writer published her early novels under an alias to avoid attention.  这位作家用化名出版早期小说以避免关注。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 nom de guerre</td><td>/ˌnɑm də ˈɡɛr/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>化名，假名</td><td>adopt a nom de guerre<br>取一个化名</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Every member of the group used a nom de guerre in letters.  小组每个成员在信中都用化名。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>putative</b></td><td>/ˈpjuːtətɪv/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>推测的，假定的</td><td>the putative author<br>推定的作者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Scholars question whether the putative author actually wrote the disputed play.  学者们怀疑那位推定的作者是否真写了这部有争议的剧本。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 assumed</td><td>/əˈsuːmd/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>假装的，假冒的</td><td>under an assumed name<br>用假名</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He traveled under an assumed name for several years.  他用假名旅行了好几年。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 ostensible</td><td>/ɑːˈstensəbl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>表面上的，佯装的</td><td>the ostensible reason<br>表面上的理由</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The ostensible purpose of the trip was research, but he went sightseeing.  这次旅行表面上的目的是研究，但他去观光了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 presumed</td><td>/prəˈzuːmd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>推定的，假定的</td><td>the presumed cause<br>推定的原因</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Faulty wiring is still the presumed cause of the fire.  线路故障仍是这场火灾的推定原因。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 reputed</td><td>/rɪˈpjuːtɪd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>据说的，号称的</td><td>the reputed owner<br>名义上的所有者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He is the reputed owner of three houses on this street.  据说这条街上有三栋房子是他的。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 seeming</td><td>/ˈsiːmɪŋ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>表面上的，貌似的</td><td>seeming indifference<br>表面上的冷淡</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her seeming calm hid a week of sleepless worry.  她表面的平静掩盖了一周的失眠焦虑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 supposed</td><td>/səˈpoʊzd/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>假定的，所谓的</td><td>the supposed benefits<br>所谓的好处</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Nobody could measure the supposed benefits of the expensive program.  没人能衡量这个昂贵项目所谓的好处。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>pecuniary</b></td><td>/pɪˈkjuːnieri/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>金钱上的</td><td>pecuniary gain<br>金钱上的收益</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The judge found that he had acted purely for pecuniary gain.  法官认定他的行为纯粹是为了金钱上的收益。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fiscal</td><td>/ˈfɪskəl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>财政的，国库的</td><td>the fiscal year<br>财政年度</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The report covers the fiscal year ending in September.  这份报告涵盖截至九月的财政年度。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 monetary</td><td>/ˈmɑːnəteri/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>货币的，金钱的</td><td>monetary policy<br>货币政策</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The bank tightened monetary policy to slow rising prices.  央行收紧货币政策以抑制物价上涨。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 financial</td><td>/fəˈnænʃəl/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>财务的，金融的</td><td>financial support<br>资金支持</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The museum survives on financial support from a few private donors.  博物馆靠少数私人捐助者的资金支持维持。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 nonfinancial</td><td>/nɑnfəˈnænʃəl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>非财务的，非金钱的</td><td>nonfinancial rewards<br>非金钱的回报</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Many volunteers stay for nonfinancial rewards such as friendship.  许多志愿者留下是为了友情这类非金钱回报。</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

## Unit 2

<table>
<thead><tr>
<th>单词</th>
<th>音标</th>
<th>常用</th>
<th>口语</th>
<th>词性</th>
<th>中文释义</th>
<th>常用短语</th>
</tr></thead>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>puckish</b></td><td>/ˈpʌkɪʃ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>淘气的，顽皮的</td><td>a puckish grin<br>顽皮的笑容</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A puckish grin spread across his face as he hid the keys.  他藏起钥匙时，脸上露出顽皮的笑容。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 devilish</td><td>/ˈdevlɪʃ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>邪恶的，恶魔般的</td><td>a devilish grin<br>邪气的笑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The boy wore a devilish grin as he hid the keys.  男孩藏起钥匙时露出一丝邪气的笑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 impish</td><td>/ˈɪmpɪʃ/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>顽皮的，淘气的</td><td>an impish grin<br>顽皮的笑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She gave an impish grin and hid the last cookie.  她顽皮地一笑，把最后一块饼干藏了起来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 prankish</del></td><td>/ˈpræŋkɪʃ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>爱开玩笑的，顽皮的</td><td>a prankish streak<br>一股顽皮劲儿</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Even at seventy he kept a prankish sense of humor.  他七十岁了还保有一种顽皮的幽默。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 waggish</del></td><td>/ˈwæɡɪʃ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>滑稽的，爱开玩笑的</td><td>a waggish grin<br>顽皮的笑容</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The waggish waiter kept teasing us about our accents.  这个爱开玩笑的服务员一直拿我们的口音逗趣。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 sober</td><td>/ˈsoʊbər/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>严肃的；节制的</td><td>a sober assessment<br>冷静的评估</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The committee offered a sober assessment of the country's economic prospects.  委员会对国家经济前景作出冷静的评估。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 grave</td><td>/ˈɡreɪv/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>严肃的，严重的；坟墓</td><td>a grave expression<br>严肃的表情</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The doctor's grave tone told us the news was bad.  医生严肃的语气告诉我们消息不好。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 staid</td><td>/ˈsteɪd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>拘谨的，古板严肃的</td><td>a staid old bank<br>古板的老银行</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The staid law firm finally allowed casual clothing.  这家古板的律所终于允许穿便装。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>puerile</b></td><td>/ˈpjʊrəl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>幼稚的，不成熟的</td><td>puerile humor<br>幼稚的幽默</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His puerile complaints annoyed the whole committee.  他幼稚的抱怨让整个委员会都不快。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 adolescent</td><td>/ˌædəˈlesənt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>青春期的；不成熟的</td><td>adolescent behavior<br>幼稚的举动</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His adolescent jokes annoyed the older members of the committee.  他幼稚的玩笑让委员会里年长的成员很不快。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 green</td><td>/ɡriːn/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>无经验的</td><td>green recruits<br>毫无经验的新兵</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The green interns needed close supervision during their very first month.  这些毫无经验的实习生在最初一个月需要密切的指导。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 immature</td><td>/ˌɪməˈtʃʊr/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>（生理、心理）未完全发展的，未发育成熟的</td><td>emotionally immature<br>情绪上不成熟</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The fruit is still immature and much too sour to pick.  这些果子还未成熟，太酸，不能采摘。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 unfledged</del></td><td>/ʌnˈflɛdʒd/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>羽毛未齐的；未成熟的</td><td>an unfledged lawyer<br>尚不老练的律师</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The unfledged birds could not yet leave the nest.  这些羽毛未齐的雏鸟还不能离巢。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 unformed</td><td>/ʌnˈfɔːrmd/</td><td>☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>未成形的，未发育完全的</td><td>unformed ideas<br>尚未成形的想法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her plan was still unformed when the meeting began.  会议开始时她的计划还没有成形。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 unripened</del></td><td>/ʌnˈraɪpənd/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>未成熟的，未熟透的</td><td>unripened green fruit<br>未熟的青果</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The unripened peaches tasted hard and unpleasantly sour.  这些没熟的桃子又硬又酸，很不好吃。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 adult</td><td>/əˈdʌlt/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>成年的，成熟的；成年人</td><td>an adult attitude<br>成熟的态度</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She handled the crisis in a calm, adult way.  她以冷静成熟的方式处理了这场危机。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 experienced</td><td>/ɪkˈspɪriənst/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>有经验的，老练的</td><td>an experienced guide<br>经验丰富的导游</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 We hired an experienced nurse to care for my grandmother.  我们请了一位有经验的护士照顾我祖母。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 grown-up</td><td>/ˈɡroʊnʌp/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>成年的；成年人</td><td>act like a grown-up<br>像成年人一样行事</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Only the grown-ups were allowed to stay for the discussion.  只有成年人被允许留下参加讨论。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 mature</td><td>/məˈtʃʊr/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词、动词</td><td>成熟的；（使）成熟</td><td>a mature judgment<br>成熟的判断</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He seems remarkably mature for a boy of fifteen.  对一个十五岁男孩来说他显得格外成熟。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 ripe</td><td>/ˈraɪp/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>成熟的，熟透的；时机成熟的</td><td>ripe for change<br>变革时机成熟</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The peaches are finally ripe enough to pick and eat.  这些桃子终于熟到可以采下来吃了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 sagacious</td><td>/səˈɡeɪʃəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>睿智的，有远见的</td><td>a sagacious adviser<br>睿智的顾问</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His sagacious advice saved the family business from certain ruin.  他睿智的建议救家族生意于必败之境。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>puissance</b></td><td>/ˈpwɪsəns/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>权力</td><td>the puissance of kings<br>君王的权势</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old chronicles celebrate the puissance of kings and their armies.  古老的编年史颂扬君王及其军队的权势。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 force</td><td>/ˈfɔːrs/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>强迫，迫使；力量</td><td>force someone to confess<br>迫使某人认罪</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The heavy rain forced them to cancel the outdoor ceremony.  大雨迫使他们取消了户外典礼。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 might</td><td>/ˈmaɪt/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>力量，威力</td><td>with all his might<br>竭尽全力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He pushed the stalled car with all his might.  他竭尽全力推那辆熄火的车。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 potency</td><td>/ˈpoʊtənsi/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>力量，效力</td><td>the potency of the drug<br>药物的效力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The vaccine loses potency if it is not kept cold.  疫苗若不冷藏就会失去效力。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 strength</td><td>/ˈstreŋkθ/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>力量，强度</td><td>regain one's strength<br>恢复体力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It took him months to regain his strength.  他花了几个月才恢复体力。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 vigor</td><td>/ˈvɪɡər/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>活力，精力</td><td>youthful vigor<br>青春活力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She argued her case with surprising vigor after a sleepless night.  一夜未眠后，她仍以惊人的活力申辩自己的主张。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 sinew</td><td>/ˈsɪnjuː/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>力量；支柱；筋；肌腱</td><td>the sinew of the nation<br>国家的力量支柱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Farmers were once called the sinew of the whole nation.  农民曾被称为整个国家的力量支柱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 powerlessness</td><td>/ˈpaʊərləsnəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>无力，无权</td><td>a sense of powerlessness<br>无力感</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Long unemployment bred a deep feeling of powerlessness.  长期失业催生了深深的无力感。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 impotence</td><td>/ˈɪmpətəns/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>无力，无能</td><td>a sense of impotence<br>无能为力之感</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Voters expressed impotence in the face of rising prices.  面对物价上涨，选民表达了无能为力之感。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 weakness</td><td>/ˈwiːknəs/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>弱点，缺点</td><td>a weakness for sweets<br>对甜食的偏好</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her main weakness is a reluctance to delegate work.  她的主要弱点是不愿把工作交给别人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del><b>pulchritude</b></del></td><td>/ˈpʌlkrətuːd/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>美丽</td><td>classical pulchritude<br>古典之美</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Poets of that age praised pulchritude far more than intelligence.  那个时代的诗人赞美容貌远多于赞美才智。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 ugliness</td><td>/ˈʌɡlinəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>丑陋，难看</td><td>the ugliness of the building<br>建筑的丑陋</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Locals still complain about the ugliness of the concrete tower.  当地人仍在抱怨那座水泥塔楼的丑陋。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 homeliness</del></td><td>/ˈhoʊmlinəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>相貌平庸；朴实</td><td>a certain homeliness<br>几分朴拙</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The portrait captures a homeliness that photographs always flattened.  这幅肖像捕捉到照片总也拍不出的朴拙。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 hideousness</td><td>/ˈhɪdiəsnəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>极其丑陋，可怕</td><td>the hideousness of the mask<br>面具的丑怖</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Children ran from the hideousness of the carved wooden mask.  孩子们被那木刻面具的丑怖吓跑了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>pulverize</b></td><td>/ˈpʌlvəraɪz/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>将…研磨成粉；将…彻底摧毁</td><td>pulverize the rock<br>把岩石磨成粉</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Workers used heavy hammers to pulverize the rock into fine powder.  工人用重锤把岩石研磨成细粉。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 solidify</td><td>/səˈlɪdəˌfaɪ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>动词</td><td>使凝固；使稳固</td><td>solidify into a mass<br>凝固成一块</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The melted wax solidified quickly in the cold room.  融化的蜡在冷房间里很快凝固了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 build</td><td>/ˈbɪld/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>建造，建立；体格</td><td>build a bridge<br>建造一座桥</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They plan to build a new library on the empty lot downtown.  他们计划在市中心的空地上建一座新图书馆。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 construct</td><td>/kənˈstrʌkt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>建造，建立；创造，想出</td><td>construct a theory<br>构建一套理论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The lawyer constructed a careful argument from a handful of ordinary documents.  律师用少量普通文件构建出一套严密的论证。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 erect</td><td>/ɪˈrekt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词、及物动词</td><td>直立的，垂直的</td><td>stand erect<br>笔直地站着</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old soldier still stood perfectly erect.  这位老兵仍站得笔直。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 put up</td><td>/pʊt ˈʌp/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>共同策划；提供；张贴</td><td>put up a scheme<br>合谋策划一个计划</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The two brothers put up the whole story to fool their teacher.  两兄弟合伙编造了整个故事来骗老师。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 raise</td><td>/ˈreɪz/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>提出（问题）；举起，提高</td><td>raise an objection<br>提出反对意见</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He raised an objection just before the vote was taken.  就在投票前他提出了反对意见。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 rear</td><td>/ˈrɪr/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>养育，培养；竖立，后部</td><td>rear a large family<br>养育一大家子</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She had to rear four children alone after her husband died.  丈夫去世后她只能独自养育四个孩子。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 set up</td><td>/sɛt ˈʌp/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>建立，创办；安装，搭建</td><td>set up a company<br>创办一家公司</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They set up a small company in the garage two years ago.  两年前他们在车库里创办了一家小公司。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>permeate</b></td><td>/ˈpɜːrmieɪt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词、不及物动词</td><td>弥漫，渗透</td><td>permeate the whole building<br>弥漫整栋楼</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The smell of fresh bread permeated the entire building by seven.  七点前，新鲜面包的香味弥漫了整栋楼。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 interpenetrate</del></td><td>/ˌɪntərˈpɛnətreɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>动词</td><td>相互渗透，贯穿</td><td>interpenetrate two cultures<br>使两种文化相互渗透</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Folk melodies and classical forms interpenetrate throughout his early symphonies.  民间旋律与古典形式在他早期交响曲中相互渗透。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 pervade</td><td>/pərˈveɪd/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>弥漫，充满</td><td>pervade the whole house<br>弥漫整座房子</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A faint smell of lavender pervaded the whole house that summer.  那个夏天，淡淡的薰衣草香弥漫了整座房子。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 suffuse</del></td><td>/səˈfjuːz/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>（色彩等）弥漫，染遍，充满</td><td>suffused with warm light<br>沐浴在暖光中</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Warm evening light suffused the quiet room with a golden glow.  傍晚的暖光把安静的房间染成金色。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 transfuse</td><td>/trænsˈfjuːz/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>注入，灌输；输血</td><td>transfuse new energy<br>注入新的活力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The young coach transfused fresh confidence into the struggling team.  这位年轻教练把新的信心注入陷入困境的球队。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>punctilious</b></td><td>/pʌŋkˈtɪliəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>注意细节的，一丝不苟的</td><td>punctilious about details<br>对细节一丝不苟</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He is punctilious about details that others simply ignore.  他对别人干脆忽略的细节一丝不苟。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 decorous</td><td>/ˈdekərəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>得体端正的</td><td>decorous behavior at dinner<br>晚餐时得体的举止</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Guests kept a decorous silence while the old bishop said grace.  老主教做餐前祷告时，客人们保持着得体的安静。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 proper</td><td>/ˈprɑːpər/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>适当的，正确的</td><td>the proper procedure<br>正确的程序</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Please store the samples in the proper containers.  请把样本存放在合适的容器里。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 starchy</td><td>/ˈstɑːrtʃi/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>拘谨古板的；含淀粉的</td><td>a starchy manner<br>拘谨古板的举止</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The new director was starchy and never joined the lunch table.  新主管很古板，从不来午餐桌上坐。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 stilted</td><td>/ˈstɪltɪd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>生硬的，不自然的</td><td>stilted conversation<br>生硬的交谈</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Their stilted conversation died out before the soup arrived.  他们生硬的交谈在汤上来前就断了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 remiss</td><td>/rɪˈmɪs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>疏忽的，不留心的</td><td>be remiss in one's duties<br>疏于职守</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 I would be remiss if I did not thank the volunteers.  如果我不感谢志愿者，那就是我的疏忽。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 slipshod</td><td>/ˈslɪpʃɑːd/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>粗心的，随意的</td><td>slipshod workmanship<br>马虎的做工</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The inspector criticized the slipshod workmanship on the newly built bridge.  检查员批评新建桥梁马虎的做工。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 casual</td><td>/ˈkæʒəwəl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>随意的，非故意的</td><td>a casual remark<br>随口的一句话</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A casual remark at dinner accidentally revealed the whole secret.  晚餐时随口一句话意外揭出了整个秘密。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 easygoing</td><td>/ˌiːziˈɡoʊɪŋ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>随和的，脾气好的</td><td>an easygoing manner<br>随和的态度</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her easygoing nature makes her popular with colleagues.  她随和的性格使她受同事欢迎。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 informal</td><td>/ɪnˈfɔːrməl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>非正式的，随意的</td><td>an informal meeting<br>非正式会议</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They settled the dispute through informal talks over coffee.  他们在咖啡桌旁通过非正式交谈解决了争端。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 laid-back</td><td>/ˈleɪd ˈbæk/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>悠闲的，随性的</td><td>a laid-back attitude<br>随性的态度</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His laid-back attitude drives the more anxious editors crazy.  他随性的态度让那些容易焦虑的编辑发疯。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unceremonious</td><td>/ˌʌnserəˈmoʊniəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>唐突无礼的，草率的</td><td>an unceremonious dismissal<br>唐突的解职</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His unceremonious exit surprised the guests still waiting for dessert.  他唐突离场，让还等着甜点的客人吃了一惊。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>pundit</b></td><td>/ˈpʌndɪt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>权威人士，专家</td><td>a television pundit<br>电视上的评论专家</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Every pundit predicted a close race and every one was wrong.  每位评论专家都预测选情接近，结果都错了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 savant</td><td>/sæˈvɑːnt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>博学之士，学者</td><td>a quiet savant<br>沉静的博学之士</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The quiet savant spent forty years cataloguing insects in a small museum.  那位沉静的博学之士在小博物馆里花了四十年为昆虫编目。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 scholar</td><td>/ˈskɑːlər/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>学者，博学之人</td><td>a distinguished scholar<br>杰出的学者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The lecture drew scholars from four different countries.  这场讲座吸引了四个国家的学者。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>pungent</b></td><td>/ˈpʌndʒənt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>（言辞）辛辣尖刻的；刺鼻的</td><td>pungent criticism<br>辛辣的批评</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His pungent criticism of the report left everyone at the table silent.  他对这份报告辛辣的批评让全桌人都沉默了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 acerbic</td><td>/əˈsɜːrbɪk/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>（语调）尖酸的，刻薄的</td><td>acerbic wit<br>尖刻的机智</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The reviewer is famous for her acerbic comments on new plays.  这位评论家以对新剧的尖酸评论闻名。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 acid</td><td>/ˈæsəd/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>尖刻的；酸的；酸</td><td>an acid remark<br>一句尖刻的话</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her acid comment about his writing silenced the whole table.  她对他文章的尖刻评论让全桌人都沉默了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 acrid</td><td>/ˈækrɪd/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>刻薄的；辛辣刺鼻的</td><td>acrid smoke<br>刺鼻的烟</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 An acrid smell of burning plastic filled the hallway.  一股塑料烧焦的刺鼻气味充满了走廊。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 caustic</td><td>/ˈkɔːstɪk/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>挖苦讽刺的，刻薄的</td><td>a caustic remark<br>刻薄的评论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His caustic remarks about the film upset the young director.  他对影片刻薄的评论让年轻导演很难过。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 mordant</td><td>/ˈmɔːrdnt/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>尖酸刻薄的</td><td>mordant wit<br>尖刻的机智</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His mordant wit amused readers but cost him several close friendships.  他尖刻的机智取悦了读者，却让他失去几段亲密友谊。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 sardonic</td><td>/sɑːrˈdɑːnɪk/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>讥讽的，嘲笑的</td><td>a sardonic smile<br>讥讽的微笑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He answered with a sardonic laugh and said nothing more.  他讥讽地笑了一声，什么也没再说。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 scalding</td><td>/ˈskɔːldɪŋ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>滚烫的；（言辞）严厉的</td><td>scalding criticism<br>严厉的批评</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The critic wrote a scalding review of the opening night.  评论家为首演之夜写了一篇严厉的评论。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 scathing</td><td>/ˈskeɪðɪŋ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>尖酸刻薄的，严厉的</td><td>a scathing attack<br>尖刻的攻击</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She made a scathing remark about his sloppy research.  她对他草率的研究作了一句尖刻的评论。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>puny</b></td><td>/ˈpjuːni/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>微小的，弱小的</td><td>a puny effort<br>微不足道的努力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Their puny effort to clean the beach barely made any difference.  他们清理海滩的微小努力几乎没有产生任何效果。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 diminutive</td><td>/dɪˈmɪnjətɪv/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>极小的</td><td>a diminutive figure<br>矮小的身影</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She noticed a diminutive figure standing quietly near the entrance.  她注意到一个小小的身影静静立在入口附近。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dwarfish</td><td>/ˈdwɔrfɪʃ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>矮小的，特小的</td><td>dwarfish pine trees<br>矮小的松树</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Only dwarfish pines survive on that windswept ridge.  只有矮小的松树能在那道风口山脊上存活。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 slight</td><td>/slaɪt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词、及物动词</td><td>轻微的，不重要的；轻视，怠慢</td><td>slight her contribution<br>轻视她的贡献</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He felt slighted when nobody mentioned his contribution during the ceremony.  典礼上无人提及他的贡献，他觉得受到了轻视。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 subnormal</td><td>/ˌsʌbˈnɔrməl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>低于正常的，低于标准的</td><td>subnormal temperatures<br>低于正常的温度</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The patient's subnormal body temperature worried the nurses.  病人低于正常的体温让护士们担心。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 enormous</td><td>/ɪˈnɔːrməs/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>巨大的，庞大的</td><td>an enormous amount of data<br>庞大的数据量</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The project required an enormous amount of money and patience.  这个项目需要巨额资金和极大耐心。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 considerable</td><td>/kənˈsɪdərəbl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>（数量上）可观（而值得注意）的</td><td>a considerable sum<br>一笔可观的钱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The renovation required a considerable amount of money and nearly two years of work.  这次翻修耗费了可观的资金和近两年的工期。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 grand</td><td>/ˈɡrænd/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>宏大的，壮丽的</td><td>a grand staircase<br>宏伟的楼梯</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They held the ceremony in a grand marble hall.  他们在宏伟的大理石厅里举行仪式。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 husky</td><td>/ˈhʌski/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>（尤指声音）沙哑的，粗糙的</td><td>a husky voice<br>沙哑的声音</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 After the long concert his voice was husky and barely audible.  漫长的演唱会后，他的声音沙哑，几乎听不见。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 king-size</td><td>/ˈkɪŋ ˌsaɪz/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>特大号的</td><td>a king-size bed<br>特大号床</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The tiny room somehow held a king-size bed and nothing else.  那间小屋居然塞进一张特大号床，别无他物。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 outsize</td><td>/ˈaʊtˌsaɪz/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>特大的，超大的</td><td>an outsize influence<br>超大的影响力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 This small country has an outsize influence on global shipping.  这个小国对全球航运有着超乎其体量的影响。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 overscale</del></td><td>/ˈoʊvərˌskeɪl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>尺度过大的</td><td>overscale furniture<br>尺寸过大的家具</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Overscale sofas make the little apartment feel even smaller.  尺寸过大的沙发让这小公寓显得更挤。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 substantial</td><td>/səbˈstænʃl/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>大量的，可观的；实质的</td><td>a substantial increase<br>大幅的增长</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The company reported a substantial increase in profits this quarter.  公司报告本季度利润大幅增长。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 whacking</td><td>/ˈwækɪŋ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>巨大的，极大的</td><td>a whacking great bill<br>一大笔账单</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The repair came with a whacking great bill we could not pay.  这次维修带来一大笔我们付不起的账单。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 whopping</td><td>/ˈwɑːpɪŋ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>巨大的，庞大的</td><td>a whopping increase<br>巨幅增长</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The film earned a whopping two billion dollars worldwide.  这部影片全球赚了惊人的二十亿美元。</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

## Unit 3

<table>
<thead><tr>
<th>单词</th>
<th>音标</th>
<th>常用</th>
<th>口语</th>
<th>词性</th>
<th>中文释义</th>
<th>常用短语</th>
</tr></thead>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>purport</b></td><td>/pərˈpɔːrt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>声称，打算</td><td>purport to be an expert<br>自称是专家</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Several websites purport to offer free medical advice from real doctors.  有几个网站声称提供真医生的免费医疗建议。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 claim</td><td>/ˈkleɪm/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>声称，自称</td><td>claim to be innocent<br>自称无辜</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He claims to have finished the whole course in one weekend.  他自称一个周末就上完了整门课。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 affirm</td><td>/əˈfɜːrm/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>肯定，断言</td><td>affirm one's belief<br>坚定表明信念</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The witness affirmed that she had seen the blue car.  证人确认她看见了那辆蓝色汽车。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 allege</td><td>/əˈledʒ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>声称，指称（未经证实）</td><td>allege serious misconduct<br>指称严重不当行为</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Prosecutors allege that the manager hid millions from investors.  检方指称该经理向投资者隐匿了数百万资金。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 assert</td><td>/əˈsɜːrt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>断言，声称；维护</td><td>assert one's innocence<br>声称自己无罪</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The defendant continued to assert his innocence throughout the trial.  被告在整个审判过程中不断声称自己无罪。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 aver</td><td>/əˈvɜːr/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>声称为真</td><td>aver that it is true<br>断言此事为真</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The witness averred that he had never met the defendant before.  证人声明他此前从未见过被告。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 avouch</del></td><td>/əˈvaʊtʃ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>断言，保证，证实</td><td>avouch the truth of it<br>保证其真实性</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Two witnesses avouched the accuracy of the merchant's account.  两名证人证实了这位商人陈述的准确性。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 avow</td><td>/əˈvaʊ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>公开承认，坦率声明</td><td>avow one's beliefs openly<br>公开表明信念</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He avowed his support for the strike in front of reporters.  他在记者面前公开表明支持这次罢工。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 profess</td><td>/prəˈfes/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>声称，假称</td><td>profess ignorance<br>声称不知情</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He professed great sympathy but offered no actual help.  他声称非常同情，却没提供实际帮助。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 deny</td><td>/dɪˈnaɪ/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>否认；拒绝给予</td><td>flatly deny the charge<br>断然否认指控</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 I do not deny that the plan carries some very real risks.  我不否认这个计划确实带有一些实实在在的风险。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 gainsay</td><td>/ˌɡeɪnˈseɪ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>否认</td><td>gainsay the evidence<br>否认证据</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 No one could gainsay the evidence the inspector had collected.  没有人能否认检查员收集到的证据。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del><b>purlieu</b></del></td><td>/ˈpɜːrluː/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>常去的地方；临近的地区</td><td>his old purlieu<br>他常去的旧地</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He returned to his old purlieu near the docks after many years.  多年之后，他回到码头附近他常去的旧地。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 haunt</td><td>/hɔːnt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>常去拜访；不断地想起，萦绕心头</td><td>haunt a favorite cafe<br>常去某咖啡馆</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 As a student he haunted the secondhand bookshops near the river.  学生时代，他常去河边的旧书店。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 rendezvous</td><td>/ˈrɑːndɪˌvuː/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>约会地点；常聚之处</td><td>a favorite rendezvous<br>常去的聚会点</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 That noisy cafe was our rendezvous every Friday evening.  那家吵闹的咖啡馆是我们每周五晚的聚会点。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 resort</td><td>/rɪˈzɔːrt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>常去之处；度假地</td><td>a summer resort<br>夏日度假地</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The lake became a busy summer resort within a decade.  这片湖十年间变成了热闹的夏日度假地。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 stamping ground</td><td>/ˈstæmpɪŋ ˈɡraʊnd/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>常去的地方，老地盘</td><td>an old stamping ground<br>旧日常去之地</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He walked me around his old stamping ground near the docks.  他带我逛了他在码头附近常去的旧地。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 stomping ground</td><td>/ˈstɑmpɪŋ ˈɡraʊnd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>常去的地方，老地盘</td><td>back on familiar stomping ground<br>回到熟悉的老地盘</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Back on her old stomping ground, she knew every shortcut.  回到熟悉的老地盘，她知道每条近路。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 backyard</td><td>/ˈbækˌjɑːrd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>附近地区；后院</td><td>in our own backyard<br>就在我们门口</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Nobody wants a waste plant in their own backyard.  谁也不想垃圾处理厂建在自家门口。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 neighborhood</td><td>/ˈneɪbərhʊd/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>邻近地区，街区</td><td>a quiet neighborhood<br>安静的街区</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They chose a quiet neighborhood with good schools nearby.  他们选了一个安静街区，附近学校也好。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 vicinage</del></td><td>/ˈvɪsɪnɪdʒ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>邻近地区，附近</td><td>in the immediate vicinage<br>紧邻的地区</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Every farm in the vicinage lost power for two days.  附近每个农场都停电了两天。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 vicinity</td><td>/vəˈsɪnəti/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>附近，邻近</td><td>in the immediate vicinity<br>在紧邻区域内</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Three witnesses were in the vicinity when the alarm sounded.  警报响时有三名证人在附近。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del><b>purloin</b></del></td><td>/pɜːrˈlɔɪn/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>偷窃</td><td>purloin a secret document<br>偷走一份密件</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The clerk managed to purloin a secret document from the locked cabinet.  那名职员设法从上锁的柜子里偷走了一份密件。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 appropriate</td><td>/əˈproʊprieɪt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>动词、形容词</td><td>私自挪用；适当的</td><td>appropriate public funds<br>挪用公款</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Officials were accused of appropriating public money for private use.  官员们被指控将公款挪为私用。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 filch</td><td>/ˈfɪltʃ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>偷取，小偷小摸</td><td>filch a few coins<br>偷走几枚硬币</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The boy filched a few coins from his father's coat pocket.  男孩从他父亲的外衣口袋里偷了几枚硬币。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 pilfer</td><td>/ˈpɪlfər/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>偷窃</td><td>pilfer office supplies<br>偷拿办公用品</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Someone had been pilfering office supplies from the storage room for months.  几个月来一直有人从储藏室偷拿办公用品。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 snitch</td><td>/ˈsnɪtʃ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>动词</td><td>偷拿；告密</td><td>snitch a cookie<br>偷拿一块饼干</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Someone snitched my pen while I was out of the room.  我离开房间时有人偷走了我的笔。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 thieve</td><td>/ˈθiːv/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>动词</td><td>偷窃，行窃</td><td>thieve from neighbors<br>偷邻居的东西</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The two brothers thieved from local shops for several months.  两兄弟连续几个月偷本地商店的东西。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>purvey</b></td><td>/pərˈveɪ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>供应，提供</td><td>purvey fresh produce<br>供应新鲜农产品</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The small shop has purveyed fresh produce to local restaurants for decades.  这家小店几十年来一直向本地餐馆供应新鲜农产品。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>pusillanimous</b></td><td>/ˌpjuːsɪˈlænɪməs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>胆小的，懦弱的</td><td>a pusillanimous response<br>软弱的回应</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Critics called the government's pusillanimous silence a betrayal of victims.  批评者称政府懦弱的沉默是对受害者的背叛。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 craven</td><td>/ˈkreɪvn/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>非常懦弱的，因胆小而遭人鄙视的</td><td>a craven retreat<br>怯懦的撤退</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His craven silence during the trial disappointed everyone who had trusted him.  他在庭审中懦弱的沉默让所有信任过他的人失望。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dastardly</td><td>/ˈdæstərdli/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>卑劣怯懦的</td><td>a dastardly trick<br>卑劣的伎俩</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Poisoning the well was a dastardly act even in wartime.  即便在战时，往井里投毒也是卑劣之举。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 gutless</td><td>/ˈɡʌtləs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>没胆量的，懦弱的</td><td>a gutless decision<br>懦弱的决定</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The team looked gutless in the second half of the match.  球队下半场表现得毫无斗志。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 poltroon</del></td><td>/pɑːlˈtruːn/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>懦夫，胆小鬼</td><td>call him a poltroon<br>骂他是懦夫</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old play calls the fleeing soldier a poltroon.  这部老戏把逃跑的士兵称作懦夫。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 recreant</del></td><td>/ˈrɛkriənt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词、形容词</td><td>背叛者，怯懦的叛徒</td><td>branded a recreant<br>被打上叛徒烙印</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The ballad calls him a recreant who fled the battle.  这首民谣称他是逃离战场的怯懦叛徒。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 spineless</td><td>/ˈspaɪnləs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>没骨气的，软弱的</td><td>a spineless coward<br>没骨气的胆小鬼</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He was too spineless to stand up to his own manager.  他太没骨气，不敢反抗自己的经理。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 brave</td><td>/ˈbreɪv/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>勇敢的</td><td>a brave decision<br>勇敢的决定</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It was brave of her to speak against the new policy.  她敢于反对新政策，很勇敢。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 courageous</td><td>/kəˈreɪdʒəs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>勇敢的，有胆量的</td><td>a courageous decision<br>勇敢的决定</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It was courageous of him to speak out against the policy.  他公开反对这项政策，很有勇气。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 daring</td><td>/ˈderɪŋ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词、形容词</td><td>勇敢，大胆</td><td>an act of daring<br>大胆之举</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Climbing that face alone took real daring and long preparation.  独自攀那面岩壁需要真正的胆量和长期准备。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 dauntless</td><td>/ˈdɔntləs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无畏的，勇敢的</td><td>dauntless courage<br>无畏的勇气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The dauntless nurse worked through shelling to reach the wounded.  这位无畏的护士在炮击中赶去救伤员。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 doughty</td><td>/ˈdaʊti/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>勇敢的，坚强的</td><td>a doughty defender<br>勇敢的捍卫者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She was a doughty defender of the village school for decades.  她数十年来一直是村校勇敢的捍卫者。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 gallant</td><td>/ˈɡælənt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>英勇的</td><td>a gallant defense<br>英勇的防守</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The gallant paratroopers jumped from the plane without a moment's hesitation.  英勇的伞兵们毫不犹豫地跳出了飞机。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 gutsy</td><td>/ˈɡʌtsi/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>有勇气的，大胆的</td><td>a gutsy performance<br>有勇气的表现</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It was a gutsy move to bet everything on one project.  把一切押在一个项目上是很有勇气的举动。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 intrepid</td><td>/ɪnˈtrepɪd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>大胆的</td><td>an intrepid explorer<br>无畏的探险家</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The intrepid explorer crossed the frozen strait alone in the middle of winter.  这位大胆的探险家在隆冬时节独自穿越了结冰的海峡。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 lionhearted</td><td>/ˈlaɪənˌhɑrtɪd/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>勇猛的，无畏的</td><td>a lionhearted captain<br>无畏的队长</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Their lionhearted goalkeeper played the second half with a broken finger.  他们无畏的守门员断了手指还打完下半场。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 stalwart</td><td>/ˈstɔːlwərt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>坚定忠诚的；强壮的</td><td>a stalwart supporter<br>坚定的支持者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She remained a stalwart supporter of the project through every setback.  历经挫折，她始终是这个项目的坚定支持者。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 stout</td><td>/ˈstaʊt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>坚定的，勇敢的；粗壮的</td><td>stout resistance<br>顽强的抵抗</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The townspeople put up a stout defense against the raiders.  镇民对袭击者进行了顽强的抵抗。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 hearted</td><td>/ˈhɑːrtəd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>有…心的（构词成分）</td><td>hearted completes stouthearted<br>拼合成原词</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Joining stout- with hearted gives back the adjective stouthearted.  把 stout- 与 hearted 接起来就还原成 stouthearted。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 valiant</td><td>/ˈvæliənt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>勇敢的，英勇的</td><td>a valiant effort<br>英勇的努力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A valiant firefighter carried two children out of the burning building.  一位英勇的消防员把两个孩子抱出了燃烧的大楼。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 valorous</td><td>/ˈvælərəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>勇猛的，英勇的</td><td>a valorous knight<br>英勇的骑士</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Songs still honor the valorous soldiers who held the bridge.  歌曲仍在颂扬守住那座桥的英勇士兵。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>quack</b></td><td>/kwæk/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>骗子医生，江湖郎中</td><td>a medical quack<br>冒牌医生</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The medical quack sold colored water as a cure for every illness.  那个江湖郎中把有色的水当作万病灵药出售。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 charlatan</td><td>/ˈʃɑːrlətən/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>骗子，装懂的人</td><td>expose a charlatan<br>揭穿骗子</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The journalist exposed the charlatan selling fake medicine.  记者揭穿了那个卖假药的骗子。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fake</td><td>/ˈfeɪk/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词、名词、动词</td><td>假的，伪造的；伪造品</td><td>a fake diploma<br>假文凭</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The gallery unknowingly displayed a fake for almost ten years.  这家画廊不知情地展出了一件赝品近十年。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fraud</td><td>/ˈfrɔːd/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>骗子；欺诈行为</td><td>expose a fraud<br>揭露骗局</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The so-called healer turned out to be an outright fraud.  那位所谓的治疗师原来是个彻头彻尾的骗子。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 hoaxer</td><td>/ˈhoʊksər/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>骗子，行骗恶作剧者</td><td>an anonymous hoaxer<br>匿名的行骗者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A hoaxer sent fake fossils to museums for nearly a decade.  一个骗子近十年间向博物馆寄送假化石。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 mountebank</td><td>/ˈmaʊntɪbæŋk/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>骗子，江湖郎中</td><td>a smooth-talking mountebank<br>口若悬河的骗子</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The town square once drew mountebanks selling miracle cures.  镇中心广场曾吸引卖神奇药方的江湖骗子。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 phony</td><td>/ˈfoʊni/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>假的，冒充的；骗子</td><td>a phony accent<br>假装的口音</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He used a phony name when renting the small apartment.  他租那间小公寓时用了假名。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 honest practitioner</td><td>/ˈɑnəst prækˈtɪʃənər/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>正派的从业者</td><td>an honest practitioner of medicine<br>正派的行医者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Every honest practitioner in town refused to sign the false report.  城里每个正派的从业者都拒绝在假报告上签字。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>quaff</b></td><td>/kwæf/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>痛饮；大口喝</td><td>quaff a mug of ale<br>大口喝下一杯麦酒</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The thirsty travelers quaffed mugs of ale at the roadside inn.  口渴的旅人在路边客栈痛饮大杯麦酒。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 gulp</td><td>/ˈɡʌlp/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>吞饮，大口地咽</td><td>gulp down the water<br>大口把水喝下</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He gulped down a glass of water and ran outside.  他大口喝下一杯水就跑了出去。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 swig</td><td>/ˈswɪɡ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>大口喝；一大口</td><td>take a swig of juice<br>大口喝一口果汁</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She took a long swig of cold juice after the run.  跑完步她大口喝了一口冰果汁。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 swill</td><td>/ˈswɪl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>动词</td><td>痛饮，大口喝；冲洗</td><td>swill down a beer<br>痛饮一杯啤酒</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They swilled cheap beer all night in the noisy bar.  他们在嘈杂的酒吧里痛饮廉价啤酒一整夜。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>quail</b></td><td>/kweɪl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>胆怯，畏缩</td><td>quail at the thought<br>一想到就发怵</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Even veteran soldiers quail at the thought of another winter campaign.  连老兵一想到又一场冬季战役也会畏缩。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 blench</td><td>/blɛntʃ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>动词</td><td>畏缩，退缩</td><td>blench at the sight<br>见状畏缩</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He did not blench at the sight of the deep wound.  看到那道深伤口他也没有畏缩。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 cringe</td><td>/ˈkrɪndʒ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>卑躬屈膝；畏缩</td><td>cringe before the boss<br>在老板面前卑躬屈膝</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The servant cringed whenever the duke raised his voice.  公爵一提高声音，仆人就畏缩起来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 recoil</td><td>/rɪˈkɔɪl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>退却，畏缩</td><td>recoil in horror<br>惊恐地退缩</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She recoiled in horror when the lamp revealed the broken door.  灯光照出被砸坏的门时，她惊恐地退缩了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 shrink</td><td>/ʃrɪŋk/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>（在数量或价值方面）降低，减小</td><td>shrink in value<br>价值缩水</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The value of the old coins began to shrink after the scandal.  丑闻之后，那些旧币的价值开始缩水。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 wince</td><td>/wɪns/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>畏缩</td><td>wince at the pain<br>因疼痛而龇牙</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She winced as the nurse cleaned the deep cut on her arm.  护士清洗她手臂上的深口子时，她疼得直畏缩。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 become resolute</td><td>/bɪˈkʌm ˈrezəluːt/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>动词</td><td>变得坚决</td><td>become resolute under pressure<br>压力下变得坚决</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Under pressure she chose to become resolute instead of giving up quietly.  在压力之下，她选择变得坚决，而不是默默放弃。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 give bold</td><td>/ɡɪv boʊld/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>给出大胆的（回应）</td><td>give bold answers<br>作出大胆回答</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Students who give bold answers learn faster than silent ones.  敢给出大胆回答的学生比沉默的学得更快。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>qualify</b></td><td>/ˈkwɑːlɪfaɪ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>限定；使有资格，使有能力</td><td>qualify for the finals<br>取得决赛资格</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The professor qualified his praise by noting several serious flaws.  教授指出几处严重缺陷，对自己的赞扬作了限定。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 equip</td><td>/ɪˈkwɪp/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>装备；使具备能力</td><td>equip students with skills<br>让学生掌握技能</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The course equips students with skills employers actually want.  这门课让学生掌握雇主真正需要的技能。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fit</td><td>/ˈfɪt/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>动词、形容词</td><td>相符，适合；合适的</td><td>fit the description<br>符合描述</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The suspect they arrested did not fit the witness's description.  他们逮捕的嫌疑人并不符合证人的描述。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 prepare</td><td>/priˈper/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>准备，预备</td><td>prepare in advance<br>提前做好准备</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The team prepared for the debate weeks in advance.  这个队伍提前数周为辩论做准备。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 ready</td><td>/ˈredi/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>及物动词、形容词</td><td>使准备好；准备好的</td><td>ready the troops<br>让部队做好准备</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Crews readied the boat while the tide was still high.  趁潮水还高，船员把船准备妥当。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 season</td><td>/ˈsiːzən/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使老练；给…调味</td><td>season young officers<br>使年轻军官老练</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Two hard winters seasoned the young crew completely.  两个艰苦的冬天把这批年轻船员彻底磨练老练了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 train</td><td>/ˈtreɪn/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>训练，培养</td><td>train new employees<br>培训新员工</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The coach trains young swimmers every morning before dawn.  教练每天黎明前训练年轻游泳选手。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unreserved</td><td>/ˌʌnrɪˈzɜrvd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>毫无保留的，无条件的</td><td>unreserved support<br>毫无保留的支持</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She offered unreserved praise for the young translator's work.  她对这位年轻译者的作品毫无保留地称赞。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 absolute</td><td>/ˈæbsəluːt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>专制的；无限的；纯净不掺杂的；确凿的</td><td>absolute power<br>绝对的权力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The king held absolute power over his subjects.  国王对臣民拥有绝对权力。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 categorical</td><td>/ˌkætəˈɡɔːrɪkl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>没有例外的；绝对的</td><td>a categorical denial<br>断然的否认</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The minister issued a categorical denial of the corruption charges.  部长断然否认了腐败指控。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>quandary</b></td><td>/ˈkwɑːndəri/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>困惑，窘境</td><td>in a real quandary<br>陷入真正的困境</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The offer of two jobs left him in a real quandary.  两份工作的邀约让他陷入真正的困境。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 catch-pp</td><td>/kætʃ ˈtwɛnti ˈtu/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>两难困境（catch-22 讹写）</td><td>a real catch-pp<br>十足的两难困境</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 This rule is a catch-pp: you need experience to gain experience.  这条规定是个两难困境：要有经验才能获得经验。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 double bind</td><td>/ˈdʌbəl ˈbaɪnd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>双重困境，两难</td><td>caught in a double bind<br>陷入双重困境</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Working parents are often caught in a double bind over childcare.  在职父母常在照看孩子上陷入双重困境。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 state of complete certainty</td><td>/steɪt ʌv kəmˈplit ˈsɜrtənti/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>完全确定的状态</td><td>a state of complete certainty<br>完全确定的状态</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Science rarely offers a state of complete certainty about anything.  科学很少对任何事给出完全确定的状态。</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

## Unit 4

<table>
<thead><tr>
<th>单词</th>
<th>音标</th>
<th>常用</th>
<th>口语</th>
<th>词性</th>
<th>中文释义</th>
<th>常用短语</th>
</tr></thead>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>quarantine</b></td><td>/ˈkwɔːrəntiːn/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>隔离</td><td>place under quarantine<br>予以隔离</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Officials placed the entire flock under quarantine after the outbreak.  疫情爆发后，官员将整群家禽予以隔离。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>qualm</b></td><td>/kwɑːm/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>担忧，不安</td><td>have no qualms about<br>对此毫无顾虑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She had no qualms about reporting the accountant to the authorities.  她对向当局举报那名会计毫无顾虑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 compunction</td><td>/kəmˈpʌŋkʃn/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>内疚，良心不安</td><td>without compunction<br>毫无内疚地</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She fired the loyal staff without the slightest compunction.  她毫无内疚地解雇了那些忠诚的员工。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 misgiving</td><td>/ˌmɪsˈɡɪvɪŋ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>担忧，疑虑</td><td>have serious misgivings<br>深感疑虑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She had serious misgivings about signing the contract without a lawyer present.  她对在没有律师在场的情况下签合同深感疑虑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 scruple</td><td>/ˈskruːpl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>（良心上的）不安</td><td>have no scruples<br>毫无顾忌</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He felt no scruple about reading the private letters of his brother.  他对偷看弟弟的私人信件毫无良心不安。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>quash</b></td><td>/kwɔːʃ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>撤销，废除；镇压，阻止</td><td>quash a conviction<br>撤销有罪判决</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The appeal court quashed his conviction last week.  上诉法院上周撤销了对他的定罪。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 repress</td><td>/rɪˈpres/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>镇压；阻止（正常的表达、活动或发展等）</td><td>repress a smile<br>忍住笑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She struggled to repress a smile during the solemn ceremony.  在庄严的仪式上她努力忍住笑意。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 squelch</td><td>/skweltʃ/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>压制，镇压（运动）</td><td>squelch the rebellion<br>镇压叛乱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Troops moved in quickly to squelch the rebellion in the province.  军队迅速开进，镇压该省的叛乱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 subdue</td><td>/səbˈduː/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使顺从；征服</td><td>subdue the rebellion<br>镇压叛乱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Government forces moved south to subdue the rebellion within a week.  政府军南下，一周内镇压了叛乱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 suppress</td><td>/səˈpres/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>用暴力终止，镇压；封锁，删除</td><td>suppress a rebellion<br>镇压叛乱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The regime used the army to suppress a rebellion in the capital.  该政权动用军队镇压首都的叛乱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 clamp down on</td><td>/klæmp ˈdaʊn ɑn/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>动词</td><td>严厉打击，压制</td><td>clamp down on speeding<br>严查超速</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The city clamped down on illegal parking near the school gates.  市里严厉整治校门附近的违章停车。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 crack down on</td><td>/kræk ˈdaʊn ɑn/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>动词</td><td>严厉打击，取缔</td><td>crack down on fraud<br>严打欺诈</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Regulators finally cracked down on the unlicensed lending apps.  监管部门终于取缔了这些无照放贷软件。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 engender</td><td>/ɪnˈdʒendər/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>引起，使发展</td><td>engender trust<br>产生信任</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Small acts of honesty engender trust more surely than grand promises do.  小小的诚实之举比宏大的承诺更能可靠地产生信任。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 foment</td><td>/foʊˈment/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>助长，煽动</td><td>foment unrest<br>煽动动乱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The pamphlets were designed to foment rebellion in the provinces.  这些小册子意在煽动各省叛乱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>quirky</b></td><td>/ˈkwɜːrki/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>古怪的，奇葩的</td><td>a quirky sense of humor<br>古怪的幽默感</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her quirky sense of humor made even dull meetings entertaining.  她古怪的幽默感让沉闷的会议也变得有趣。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 bizarre</td><td>/bəˈzɑːr/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>怪异的，离奇的</td><td>a bizarre coincidence<br>离奇的巧合</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 By some bizarre coincidence we wore the same coat.  出于某种离奇的巧合，我们穿了同款外套。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 eccentric</td><td>/ɪkˈsentrɪk/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>古怪的，不循规蹈矩的</td><td>an eccentric old professor<br>行为古怪的老教授</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 An eccentric old professor kept three clocks that all showed different times.  一位行为古怪的老教授留着三只指着不同时间的钟。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 erratic</td><td>/ɪˈrætɪk/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>反复无常的，不规律的</td><td>erratic driving behavior<br>飘忽不定的驾驶行为</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His erratic driving behavior alarmed the passengers long before the police stopped him.  早在警察拦下他之前，他飘忽不定的驾驶行为就让乘客惊慌。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 outlandish</td><td>/aʊtˈlændɪʃ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>古怪的，奇异的</td><td>outlandish conspiracy theories<br>离奇的阴谋论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her outlandish hat made every head turn as she entered the hall.  她走进大厅时，那顶古怪的帽子让所有人回头。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 peculiar</td><td>/pəˈkjuːliər/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>奇怪的；特有的</td><td>a peculiar smell<br>一股怪味</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 There was a peculiar smell coming from the basement.  地下室传来一股奇怪的味道。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 odd</td><td>/ˈɑːd/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>奇怪的；奇数的</td><td>an odd pair of socks<br>不成对的袜子</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It seemed odd that nobody answered the door at noon.  中午没人应门，这显得有点奇怪。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 strange</td><td>/ˈstreɪndʒ/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>奇怪的；陌生的</td><td>a strange noise upstairs<br>楼上奇怪的声响</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 There was a strange smell coming from the kitchen last night.  昨晚厨房里传出一股奇怪的味道。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 weird</td><td>/ˈwɪrd/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>怪异的，离奇的</td><td>a weird coincidence<br>离奇的巧合</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The film has a weird ending that nobody in the theater understood.  这部电影的结尾很怪，影院里没人看懂。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>quell</b></td><td>/kwel/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>压制；使平静，使安静</td><td>quell the unrest<br>平息动乱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Troops were sent in to quell the growing unrest.  部队被派去平息日益扩大的动乱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 quash</td><td>/kwɔːʃ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>撤销，废除；镇压，阻止</td><td>quash a conviction<br>撤销有罪判决</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The appeal court quashed his conviction last week.  上诉法院上周撤销了对他的定罪。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 repress</td><td>/rɪˈpres/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>镇压；阻止（正常的表达、活动或发展等）</td><td>repress a smile<br>忍住笑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She struggled to repress a smile during the solemn ceremony.  在庄严的仪式上她努力忍住笑意。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 squash</td><td>/ˈskwɑːʃ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>镇压，压制；压扁</td><td>squash the rumor<br>压下谣言</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The company quickly squashed the rumor about layoffs.  公司迅速压下了关于裁员的谣言。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 squelch</td><td>/skweltʃ/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>压制，镇压（运动）</td><td>squelch the rebellion<br>镇压叛乱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Troops moved in quickly to squelch the rebellion in the province.  军队迅速开进，镇压该省的叛乱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 subdue</td><td>/səbˈduː/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使顺从；征服</td><td>subdue the rebellion<br>镇压叛乱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Government forces moved south to subdue the rebellion within a week.  政府军南下，一周内镇压了叛乱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 suppress</td><td>/səˈpres/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>用暴力终止，镇压；封锁，删除</td><td>suppress a rebellion<br>镇压叛乱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The regime used the army to suppress a rebellion in the capital.  该政权动用军队镇压首都的叛乱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 clamp down on</td><td>/klæmp ˈdaʊn ɑn/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>动词</td><td>严厉打击，压制</td><td>clamp down on speeding<br>严查超速</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The city clamped down on illegal parking near the school gates.  市里严厉整治校门附近的违章停车。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 crack down on</td><td>/kræk ˈdaʊn ɑn/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>动词</td><td>严厉打击，取缔</td><td>crack down on fraud<br>严打欺诈</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Regulators finally cracked down on the unlicensed lending apps.  监管部门终于取缔了这些无照放贷软件。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dumb</td><td>/dʌm/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>愚蠢的；不会说话的</td><td>a dumb question<br>愚蠢的问题</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Asking that in public was a pretty dumb idea.  在公开场合问那个是个相当愚蠢的想法。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 extinguish</td><td>/ɪkˈstɪŋɡwɪʃ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>熄灭</td><td>extinguish the fire<br>扑灭大火</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Firefighters worked all night to extinguish the burning warehouse.  消防员整夜工作扑灭燃烧的仓库。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 hush</td><td>/ˈhʌʃ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>使安静，平息</td><td>hush the crying baby<br>哄哭闹的婴儿安静</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She hushed the restless children with one long story.  她用一个长故事把躁动的孩子们哄安静了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 mute</td><td>/mjuːt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不说话的，缄默的</td><td>remain mute on the issue<br>对此事保持缄默</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He remained mute on the issue despite repeated questions from reporters.  尽管记者反复提问，他对此事仍保持沉默。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 settle</td><td>/ˈsetəl/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>解决，平息；定居</td><td>settle the dispute<br>解决争端</td></tr>

</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 foment</td><td>/foʊˈment/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>助长，煽动</td><td>foment unrest<br>煽动动乱</td></tr>

<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 instigate</td><td>/ˈɪnstɪɡeɪt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>煽动，激起</td><td>instigate a rebellion<br>煽动叛乱</td></tr>

</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 rouse</td><td>/raʊz/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>激起，煽动；唤醒</td><td>rouse public anger<br>激起公众愤怒</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The speech was meant to rouse public anger against the proposed tax.  那场演讲意在激起公众对拟征税款的愤怒。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 incite</td><td>/ɪnˈsaɪt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>煽动，激起</td><td>incite a riot<br>煽动暴乱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He was charged with inciting a riot outside the county courthouse.  他被指控在县法院外煽动暴乱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>quench</b></td><td>/kwentʃ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>解（渴）；熄灭</td><td>quench one's thirst<br>解渴</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A glass of cold lemonade quickly quenched my desperate thirst.  一杯冰镇柠檬水很快解了我的焦渴。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 blanket</td><td>/ˈblæŋkət/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>覆盖以扑灭；毯子</td><td>blanket the flames<br>覆盖扑灭火焰</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Firefighters blanketed the burning oil with thick white foam.  消防员用厚厚的白色泡沫覆盖扑灭了燃烧的油。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 douse</td><td>/daʊs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>扑灭（火或灯）；浇透</td><td>douse the flames<br>扑灭火焰</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Firefighters worked all night to douse the flames on the upper floors.  消防员整夜奋战，扑灭了高层的火焰。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 put out</td><td>/ˈpʊt ˈaʊt/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>使不便，麻烦；扑灭</td><td>put you out<br>麻烦到你</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 I hope my early arrival did not put you out too much.  希望我来得早没太麻烦你。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 snuff out</td><td>/snʌf ˈaʊt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>扑灭；终止，毁灭</td><td>snuff out a candle<br>掐灭蜡烛</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A single storm can snuff out years of careful planting.  一场风暴就能毁掉多年的精心种植。</td></tr>

<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 assuage</td><td>/əˈsweɪdʒ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>缓和，减轻；平息</td><td>assuage his fears<br>减轻他的恐惧</td></tr>

</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 sate</td><td>/seɪt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使饱足，充分满足</td><td>sate one's hunger<br>充分满足饥饿感</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A single bowl of thin soup was not enough to sate his hunger.  一碗清汤不足以满足他的饥饿。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 satiate</td><td>/ˈseɪʃieɪt/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词、形容词</td><td>（使）饱足（的），过分满足（的）</td><td>satiate his curiosity<br>充分满足他的好奇心</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 No amount of reading could satiate the curiosity of that boy about stars.  再多的阅读也无法满足那男孩对星星的好奇。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 slake</td><td>/sleɪk/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>解渴；满足（欲望）</td><td>slake one's thirst<br>解渴</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They stopped at the well to slake their thirst after the long climb.  长途攀登后他们在井边停下解渴。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 fire</td><td>/ˈfaɪər/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>解雇；开火；火</td><td>fire an idle worker<br>解雇懒散的员工</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The manager fired two clerks after the audit found losses.  审计查出亏损后经理解雇了两名职员。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 ignite</td><td>/ɪɡˈnaɪt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>点燃，使燃烧；激起（感情）</td><td>ignite public outrage<br>激起公众愤怒</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The verdict ignited public outrage across the entire country within a few hours.  判决在几小时内就激起了全国的公众愤怒。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 inflame</td><td>/ɪnˈfleɪm/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使加剧</td><td>inflame public anger<br>使公众愤怒加剧</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The mayor's careless comments only inflamed public anger over the housing shortage.  市长轻率的言论只是加剧了公众对住房短缺的愤怒。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 kindle</td><td>/ˈkɪndl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>点燃</td><td>kindle a fire<br>点燃火堆</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A single spark can kindle a dangerous forest fire.  一颗火星就能点燃危险的森林大火。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 light</td><td>/ˈlaɪt/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>轻微的，不严重的</td><td>a light meal<br>一顿便餐</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He suffered only light injuries in the accident.  他在事故中只受了轻伤。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>querulous</b></td><td>/ˈkwerələs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>抱怨的，爱发牢骚的</td><td>a querulous tone<br>抱怨的语调</td></tr>

</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 crabby</td><td>/ˈkræbi/</td><td>★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>易怒的，脾气坏的</td><td>a crabby old man<br>脾气坏的老头</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He gets crabby whenever he misses his afternoon nap.  他一没睡午觉就变得烦躁易怒。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 cranky</td><td>/ˈkræŋki/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>脾气坏的，易怒的</td><td>a cranky toddler<br>闹脾气的幼儿</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The baby was cranky all night because of the heat.  因为天热，宝宝整夜都在闹脾气。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 grouchy</td><td>/ˈɡraʊtʃi/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>脾气坏的，爱抱怨的</td><td>grouchy before coffee<br>喝咖啡前脾气差</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He is grouchy every morning until the second cup of coffee.  每天早上第二杯咖啡之前他都很暴躁。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 grumpy</td><td>/ˈɡrʌmpi/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>脾气坏的，爱发牢骚的</td><td>a grumpy old neighbor<br>爱发牢骚的老邻居</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Our grumpy neighbor complains whenever the children play outside.  孩子们一在外面玩，爱发牢骚的邻居就抱怨。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 forbearing</td><td>/fɔrˈbɛrɪŋ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>宽容的，有耐心的</td><td>a forbearing teacher<br>宽容的老师</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His forbearing manner calmed even the rudest customers.  他宽容的态度连最无礼的顾客都能安抚。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 long-suffering</td><td>/ˈlɔŋ ˈsʌfərɪŋ/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>长期忍耐的</td><td>his long-suffering wife<br>长期忍耐的妻子</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His long-suffering assistant rewrote the report for the fourth time.  他长期忍耐的助手第四次重写了那份报告。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 patient</td><td>/ˈpeɪʃənt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>有耐心的，能忍耐的</td><td>patient with beginners<br>对新手有耐心</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A good teacher stays patient with beginners who ask the same question.  好老师对反复问同一问题的新手仍有耐心。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 stoic</td><td>/ˈstoʊɪk/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>隐忍的，冷静的</td><td>stoic acceptance of pain<br>隐忍地承受痛苦</td></tr>
