</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 tolerant</td><td>/ˈtɑːlərənt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>宽容的，容忍的</td><td>tolerant of criticism<br>能容忍批评</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A good editor is tolerant of odd but honest writing.  好编辑对古怪却真诚的文字是宽容的。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 uncomplaining</td><td>/ˌʌnkəmˈpleɪnɪŋ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不抱怨的，能忍耐的</td><td>uncomplaining hard work<br>不吭一声地苦干</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She was uncomplaining even through the longest night shifts.  即使最长的夜班她也从不抱怨。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>quibble</b></td><td>/ˈkwɪbl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>不及物动词、名词</td><td>吹毛求疵</td><td>quibble over details<br>在细节上挑刺</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They quibbled over the wording while the deadline slipped away.  他们在措辞上纠缠不休，截止日期却悄悄过去。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 carp</td><td>/kɑːrp/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>对小事吹毛求疵，挑剔，表现不满</td><td>carp at every detail<br>对每个细节都挑剔</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She carped at every detail of the seating arrangement.  她对座位安排的每个细节都挑剔。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 cavil</td><td>/ˈkævl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>动词</td><td>挑剔，吹毛求疵</td><td>cavil over wording<br>在措辞上吹毛求疵</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Reviewers caviled over the wording instead of judging the ideas.  评审者在措辞上吹毛求疵，而不评判观点。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fuss</td><td>/fʌs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词、不及物动词</td><td>小题大做，抱怨；忙乱</td><td>make a fuss about<br>为…大惊小怪</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Don't make a fuss about a tiny stain on the tablecloth.  别为桌布上一小块污渍小题大做。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 niggle</td><td>/ˈnɪɡəl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>对琐事挑剔；使烦扰</td><td>niggle over trifles<br>为琐事吹毛求疵</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Reviewers niggled over commas instead of judging the argument.  评审者在逗号上挑剔，却不评判论点本身。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 nitpick</td><td>/ˈnɪtpɪk/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>动词</td><td>吹毛求疵</td><td>nitpick about grammar<br>在语法上吹毛求疵</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Reviewers nitpick about grammar while ignoring the serious flaws in the argument.  评审者在语法上吹毛求疵，却忽视论证中的严重缺陷。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>quiescent</b></td><td>/kwiˈesnt/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>平静的，静止的</td><td>a quiescent period<br>静止期</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The volcano has remained quiescent for more than two hundred years.  这座火山已经平静了两百多年。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dull</td><td>/dʌl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>迟钝的；乏味的</td><td>a dull student<br>迟钝的学生</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The lecture was so dull that half the class slept.  讲座乏味得让一半学生睡着了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 inert</td><td>/ɪˈnɜːrt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>（人）懒惰缺乏活力的；（物品）惰性的</td><td>an inert gas<br>惰性气体</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Helium is an inert gas, so it rarely reacts with other elements.  氦是一种惰性气体，因此很少与其他元素发生反应。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 lethargic</td><td>/ləˈθɑːrdʒɪk/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>没精打采的，行动迟缓的</td><td>feel lethargic all day<br>整天没精打采</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The heat left everyone lethargic and unwilling to move before evening.  酷热让所有人都没精打采，天黑前都不愿动。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 sluggish</td><td>/ˈslʌɡɪʃ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>行动迟缓的，懒散的</td><td>feel sluggish all morning<br>整个上午都懒洋洋</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He felt sluggish all morning and could barely finish his simplest chores.  他整个上午都懒洋洋的，连最简单的杂事都几乎做不完。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 torpid</td><td>/ˈtɔːrpɪd/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>迟钝的，无生气的</td><td>a torpid winter afternoon<br>慵懒迟滞的冬日下午</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The heat left the whole village torpid and unwilling to move.  酷热让整个村子都变得麻木迟钝，不愿动弹。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 rambunctious</td><td>/ræmˈbʌŋkʃəs/</td><td>★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>喧闹失控的，撒欢的</td><td>rambunctious children<br>闹得没边的孩子</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Three rambunctious puppies knocked over the coffee table.  三只闹腾的小狗把茶几撞翻了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 tumultuous</td><td>/tuˈmʌltʃuəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>喧闹的；动乱的</td><td>tumultuous applause<br>雷鸣般的掌声</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The pianist returned to tumultuous applause from the standing crowd.  钢琴家返场时全场起立，掌声雷动。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 active</td><td>/ˈæktɪv/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>活跃的，活动的</td><td>an active volcano<br>活火山</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She stays active by swimming every morning.  她每天早上游泳以保持活跃。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 restlessly active</td><td>/ˈrɛstləsli ˈæktɪv/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>躁动不安的</td><td>a restlessly active mind<br>躁动不停的头脑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His restlessly active mind jumped from project to project.  他躁动不停的头脑在各个项目间跳跃。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>quixotic</b></td><td>/kwɪkˈsɑːtɪk/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不切实际的，空想的</td><td>a quixotic quest<br>不切实际的追寻</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His quixotic quest to reform the entire system ended in failure.  他要改革整个体制的不切实际的追寻以失败告终。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 impractical</td><td>/ˌɪmˈpræktəkəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不切实际的</td><td>an impractical plan<br>不切实际的计划</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Building a bridge there proved impractical and hugely expensive.  在那里建桥被证明不切实际且极其昂贵。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 ideal</td><td>/aɪˈdiːl/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词、形容词</td><td>理想，典范；理想的</td><td>an ideal solution<br>理想的解决办法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She remains the ideal of quiet courage for many students.  她仍是许多学生心中沉静勇气的典范。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 romantic</td><td>/roʊˈmæntɪk/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不切实际的；浪漫的</td><td>a romantic notion<br>浪漫而不实的想法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He has a romantic notion that farming is always peaceful.  他有个浪漫想法，以为农耕总是宁静的。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 starry</td><td>/ˈstɑri/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>梦幻不实的；布满星的</td><td>starry hopes<br>梦幻般的期望</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her starry plans for the theater never survived the budget meeting.  她对剧院的梦幻计划没能通过预算会议。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 utopian</td><td>/juˈtoʊpiən/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>乌托邦式的，空想的</td><td>a utopian scheme<br>空想的方案</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The pamphlet proposed a utopian city without money or locks.  这本小册子设想了一座没有钱和锁的空想城市。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 visionary</td><td>/ˈvɪʒəneri/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>有远见的；不切实际的，空想的</td><td>a visionary founder<br>有远见的创始人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Their visionary founder predicted the mobile market twenty years early.  他们有远见的创始人提前二十年预见了移动市场。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 clear-eyed</td><td>/ˌklɪr ˈaɪd/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>看得清楚的，冷静清醒的</td><td>a clear-eyed assessment<br>清醒的评估</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 We need a clear-eyed view of the risks before investing.  投资前我们需要对风险有清醒的认识。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 clear-sighted</td><td>/ˌklɪr ˈsaɪtɪd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>有眼光的，有洞察力的</td><td>a clear-sighted leader<br>有远见的领导者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Only a clear-sighted planner could foresee the coming water shortage.  只有有眼光的规划者才能预见即将到来的缺水。</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

## Unit 5

<table>
<thead><tr>
<th>单词</th>
<th>音标</th>
<th>常用</th>
<th>口语</th>
<th>词性</th>
<th>中文释义</th>
<th>常用短语</th>
</tr></thead>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>quota</b></td><td>/ˈkwoʊtə/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>配额，限额</td><td>meet the sales quota<br>完成销售配额</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The team worked overtime to meet the monthly sales quota.  团队加班以完成每月的销售配额。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 allowance</td><td>/əˈlaʊəns/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>限额；津贴</td><td>a baggage allowance<br>行李限额</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Each passenger has a baggage allowance of two small bags.  每位乘客的行李限额是两个小包。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 portion</td><td>/ˈpɔːrʃən/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>一份，部分</td><td>a generous portion<br>分量很足的一份</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They served a generous portion of rice with every dish.  每道菜都配了分量很足的米饭。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 proportion</td><td>/prəˈpɔːrʃən/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>比例，份额</td><td>a large proportion of students<br>很大比例的学生</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A large proportion of the budget goes to teacher salaries.  预算中很大比例用于教师工资。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unlimited number</td><td>/ʌnˈlɪmɪtɪd ˈnʌmbər/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>无限的数量</td><td>an unlimited number of tries<br>无限次尝试</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The app allows an unlimited number of tries before the test.  这个应用允许考前无限次尝试。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>quotidian</b></td><td>/kwoʊˈtɪdiən/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>每日的；平凡的</td><td>quotidian routine<br>日常的例行事务</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The novel finds real beauty in the quotidian routine of village life.  这部小说在乡村生活的日常事务中发现了真正的美。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 commonplace</td><td>/ˈkɑːmənpleɪs/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>平凡的；老生常谈</td><td>a commonplace observation<br>老生常谈的看法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Video calls have become commonplace in ordinary office life.  视频通话在日常办公中已很平常。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 everyday</td><td>/ˈevrideɪ/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>日常的，平常的</td><td>everyday household chores<br>日常家务</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The book finds poetry in everyday chores like washing dishes.  这本书在洗碗一类日常家务里找到诗意。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 frequent</td><td>/ˈfriːkwənt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>频繁的，经常的</td><td>frequent visits<br>频繁的探访</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Frequent power cuts made it hard to finish the work.  频繁停电让工作难以完成。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 ordinary</td><td>/ˈɔːrdəneri/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>普通的，平常的</td><td>just an ordinary weekday<br>只是平常的工作日</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He looked like an ordinary commuter waiting for the morning bus.  他看上去就像个等早班公交的普通上班族。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 routine</td><td>/ruˈtiːn/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>例行的；常规程序</td><td>a routine check<br>例行检查</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The problem was discovered during a routine inspection last week.  这个问题是上周例行检查时发现的。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 ubiquitous</td><td>/juːˈbɪkwɪtəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无所不在的，普遍存在的</td><td>ubiquitous smartphones<br>随处可见的手机</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Cheap sensors are now ubiquitous in cars, phones, and doorbells.  廉价传感器如今在汽车、手机和门铃里无处不在。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 remarkable</td><td>/rɪˈmɑːrkəbəl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>非凡的，引人注目的</td><td>a remarkable achievement<br>非凡的成就</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Finishing the bridge in a year was a remarkable achievement.  一年建成这座桥是非凡的成就。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 striking</td><td>/ˈstraɪkɪŋ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>引人注目的，显著的</td><td>a striking resemblance<br>惊人的相似</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 There is a striking contrast between the two sisters' styles.  两姐妹的风格形成鲜明的对比。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 extraordinary</td><td>/ɪkˈstrɔːrdəneri/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>非凡的，特别的</td><td>extraordinary courage<br>非凡的勇气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It took extraordinary courage to report what she had seen.  举报她所见之事需要非凡的勇气。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unusual</td><td>/ʌnˈjuːʒuəl/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不寻常的，罕见的</td><td>an unusual coincidence<br>罕见的巧合</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It was an unusual coincidence that both letters arrived together.  两封信同时到达是个罕见的巧合。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 rare</td><td>/ˈrer/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>稀少的，罕见的</td><td>a rare bird<br>罕见的鸟</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It is rare to see snow here in April.  四月这里下雪很罕见。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 infrequent</td><td>/ˌɪnˈfriːkwənt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>稀少的，不常发生的</td><td>infrequent visits<br>难得的探访</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His visits home became increasingly infrequent.  他回家的次数越来越少。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 seldom</td><td>/ˈseldəm/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>副词</td><td>很少，不常</td><td>seldom if ever<br>几乎从不</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He seldom answers the phone before ten in the morning.  他早上十点前很少接电话。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>rabble</b></td><td>/ˈræbl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>混乱的人群；暴民；下层民众</td><td>stir up the rabble<br>煽动暴民</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The speaker tried to stir up the rabble outside the courthouse.  演讲者试图煽动法院外的暴民。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 ragtag and bobtail</td><td>/ˈræɡtæɡ ənd ˈbɑbteɪl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>乌合之众，下层民众</td><td>the ragtag and bobtail<br>一群乌合之众</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old chronicle dismisses the marchers as ragtag and bobtail.  旧编年史把这些游行者贬为乌合之众。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 riffraff</td><td>/ˈrɪˌfræf/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>下层民众，乌合之众</td><td>keep out the riffraff<br>把闲杂人挡在外面</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The club raised its prices to keep out the riffraff.  俱乐部提价，把闲杂人等挡在门外。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 scum</td><td>/ˈskʌm/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>人渣，社会渣滓</td><td>the scum of the earth<br>社会的渣滓</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The guards treated every prisoner as the scum of the earth.  看守把每个囚犯都当成社会渣滓对待。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 rag</td><td>/ˈræɡ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>乌合之众；破布</td><td>rag, tag, and bobtail<br>乌合之众</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old phrase rag, tag, and bobtail simply meant the common crowd.  古语 rag, tag, and bobtail 就是指普通民众。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 trash</td><td>/ˈtræʃ/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>下层败类；垃圾</td><td>dismiss them as trash<br>视其为下层败类</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her letters show real contempt, calling the newcomers trash.  她的信里满是轻蔑，把新来者称作下层败类。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 unwashed</td><td>/ənˈwɑːʃt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>平民大众的；未洗的</td><td>the great unwashed<br>平民大众</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Editorials of that era sneered at the great unwashed.  那个时代的社论嘲讽平民大众。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>rabid</b></td><td>/ˈræbɪd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>狂热的，不冷静的</td><td>a rabid supporter<br>狂热的支持者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A rabid supporter of the team shouted insults at the referee.  一个狂热的球队支持者对裁判大声辱骂。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 delirious</td><td>/dɪˈlɪriəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>神志昏乱的；极度兴奋的</td><td>delirious with fever<br>因发烧而神志不清</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The patient was delirious and kept calling out strange names.  病人神志不清，不停地喊出奇怪的名字。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 ferocious</td><td>/fəˈroʊʃəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>激烈的，爆发性的；凶猛的，残暴的</td><td>a ferocious storm<br>猛烈的风暴</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A ferocious storm tore the roof off the fishing shed.  一场猛烈的风暴把渔棚的屋顶掀掉了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 feverish</td><td>/ˈfiːvərɪʃ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>狂热的；发烧的</td><td>feverish activity<br>狂热的忙乱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 There was feverish activity backstage an hour before curtain.  开演前一小时后台一片狂热忙乱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fierce</td><td>/ˈfɪrs/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>凶猛的；激烈的</td><td>fierce competition<br>激烈的竞争</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The two teams faced fierce competition for the final spot.  两队为最后一个名额展开激烈竞争。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 frantic</td><td>/ˈfræntɪk/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>狂乱的，急切的</td><td>a frantic search<br>疯狂的搜寻</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A frantic search for the missing keys delayed everyone.  一场疯狂找钥匙的搜寻让大家都晚了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 frenetic</td><td>/frəˈnetɪk/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>狂热的，狂乱的</td><td>a frenetic pace<br>疯狂的节奏</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The kitchen keeps a frenetic pace from six until midnight.  厨房从六点到午夜都保持疯狂的节奏。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 violent</td><td>/ˈvaɪələnt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>猛烈的；暴力的</td><td>a violent reaction<br>剧烈的反应</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Mixing the two powders causes a violent reaction.  把这两种粉末混合会引起剧烈反应。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 logical</td><td>/ˈlɑːdʒɪkəl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>合乎逻辑的，合理的</td><td>a logical conclusion<br>合乎逻辑的结论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Selling the old machines was the logical next step.  卖掉旧机器是顺理成章的下一步。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 detached</td><td>/dɪˈtætʃt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>冷漠超然的，不介入的</td><td>a detached attitude<br>超然冷漠的态度</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She described the accident in a strangely detached voice.  她用一种异常冷漠的语气描述了那场事故。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>racy</b></td><td>/ˈreɪsi/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>活泼生动的</td><td>a racy narrative style<br>生动的叙述风格</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her racy narrative style keeps readers turning pages late into the night.  她生动活泼的叙述风格让读者深夜还在翻页。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 animate</td><td>/ˈænəmeɪt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词、形容词</td><td>使有生气，激励；有活力的</td><td>animate the discussion<br>让讨论活跃起来</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A few well-chosen questions can animate an otherwise dull discussion.  几个精心挑选的问题就能让本来沉闷的讨论活跃起来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 brisk</td><td>/brɪsk/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>充满生机的，有活力的</td><td>a brisk walk<br>快步散步</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A brisk walk before breakfast leaves me alert all morning.  早餐前快走一趟能让我整个上午都很清醒。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 frisky</td><td>/ˈfrɪski/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>活泼欢腾的</td><td>a frisky puppy<br>一只活泼的小狗</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The frisky colt raced across the field at dawn.  黎明时那匹活泼的小马驹在田野上奔跑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 jaunty</td><td>/ˈdʒɔːnti/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>轻快的，活泼的</td><td>a jaunty stride<br>轻快的步伐</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He walked into the office with a jaunty step and a broad smile.  他步伐轻快、笑容满面地走进了办公室。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 perky</td><td>/ˈpɜːrki/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>活泼的，精神饱满的</td><td>a perky waitress<br>活泼的女服务员</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She sounded surprisingly perky for six in the morning.  早上六点，她听起来却出奇地精神。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 spirited</td><td>/ˈspɪrɪtɪd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>生气勃勃的，活跃的</td><td>a spirited debate<br>一场热烈的辩论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The candidates held a spirited debate about tax policy.  候选人就税收政策展开了热烈的辩论。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 vivacious</td><td>/vɪˈveɪʃəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>活泼的，快活的</td><td>a vivacious personality<br>活泼的性格</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her vivacious manner made every stranger feel welcome at the table.  她活泼的举止让每个陌生人在餐桌旁都感到受欢迎。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 tame</td><td>/teɪm/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词、及物动词</td><td>被驯化的；控制，抑制</td><td>tame the wild horse<br>驯服野马</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It took the trainer months to tame the wild horse completely.  训马师花了几个月才彻底驯服那匹野马。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 dead</td><td>/ˈded/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>死的；毫无生气的</td><td>a dead town at night<br>夜里死寂的小镇</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The resort is completely dead in the winter months.  这个度假地在冬季几个月里毫无生气。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 inactive</td><td>/ɪnˈæktɪv/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不活动的，不活跃的</td><td>remain inactive for months<br>数月毫无动作</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The volcano has been inactive for over two hundred years.  这座火山已有两百多年没有活动。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 inanimate</td><td>/ɪnˈænɪmət/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无生命的</td><td>an inanimate object<br>无生命的物体</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Children often speak to inanimate objects as though they could reply.  孩子常对无生命的物体说话，仿佛它们能够回答。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 lackadaisical</td><td>/ˌlækəˈdeɪzɪkəl/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>懒散的，无兴趣的</td><td>a lackadaisical attitude<br>懒散的态度</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His lackadaisical attitude toward homework worried both parents.  他对作业懒散的态度让父母都很担心。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 languid</td><td>/ˈlæŋɡwɪd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>没精打采的，虚弱的</td><td>a languid summer afternoon<br>慢悠悠的夏日午后</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A languid summer afternoon passed with nobody willing to leave the shade.  一个慢悠悠的夏日午后过去了，没人愿意离开树荫。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 languorous</td><td>/ˈlæŋɡərəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>倦怠的，无精打采的</td><td>a languorous afternoon<br>倦懒的午后</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They spent a languorous afternoon lying under the fig tree.  他们在无花果树下度过一个倦懒的午后。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 leaden</td><td>/ˈledən/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>沉重的，无生气的；铅灰的</td><td>a leaden sky<br>铅灰色的天空</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He walked home with leaden steps after the painful defeat.  痛苦的失败后，他步履沉重地走回家。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 lifeless</td><td>/ˈlaɪfləs/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>无生命的；无生气的</td><td>a lifeless performance<br>毫无生气的表演</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The actors gave a lifeless performance to a bored audience.  演员们在无聊的观众前演得毫无生气。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 limp</td><td>/lɪmp/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>不及物动词、形容词</td><td>跛行，艰难缓慢地走；柔软的</td><td>limp toward the door<br>跛行走向门口</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He limped slowly toward the door with his ankle wrapped in bandages.  他脚踝缠着绷带，一步一跛地慢慢走向门口。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 listless</td><td>/ˈlɪstləs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>无精打采的，倦怠的</td><td>feel listless and tired<br>感到倦怠疲乏</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The fever left her listless for nearly a week.  发烧让她将近一周都无精打采。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 spiritless</del></td><td>/ˈspɪrɪtləs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无精打采的，没有生气的</td><td>a spiritless performance<br>毫无生气的表演</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The team gave a spiritless performance in the second half.  球队在下半场表现得毫无生气。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 vapid</td><td>/ˈvæpɪd/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>无趣的，乏味的</td><td>a vapid conversation<br>乏味的谈话</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The party ended in vapid conversation about traffic and the weather.  聚会最后只剩下关于交通和天气的乏味闲谈。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>raffish</b></td><td>/ˈræfɪʃ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>低俗的；放浪不羁的</td><td>a raffish charm<br>低俗却迷人的味道</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old actor had a raffish charm that everyone found irresistible.  这位老演员有种放浪不羁的魅力，人人难以抗拒。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 crass</td><td>/kræs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>粗俗的，愚钝的</td><td>crass commercialism<br>赤裸裸的商业气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His crass joke silenced the entire table at dinner.  他粗俗的玩笑让整桌人都沉默了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 lowbred</del></td><td>/ˈloʊˌbrɛd/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>粗俗的，没教养的</td><td>lowbred manners<br>没教养的举止</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The hostess found his loud jokes lowbred and tiresome.  女主人觉得他大声的玩笑粗俗又乏味。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 uncouth</td><td>/ʌnˈkuːθ/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>粗鲁的，无教养的</td><td>uncouth table manners<br>粗野的餐桌举止</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His uncouth remarks at dinner offended nearly everyone at the table.  他在晚餐上粗鲁的言辞几乎惹恼了同桌所有人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 uncultivated</td><td>/ʌnˈkʌltɪveɪtɪd/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>未开垦的；未受教养的</td><td>uncultivated soil<br>未开垦的土壤</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Much of the island remains uncultivated and covered in scrub.  岛上大部分地方仍未开垦，长满灌木。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 uncultured</td><td>/ʌnˈkʌltʃərd/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>没有教养的，粗俗的</td><td>an uncultured remark<br>粗俗的言论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They dismissed him as uncultured because of his accent.  他们因他的口音就认定他没教养。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 unpolished</td><td>/ʌnˈpɑlɪʃt/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>粗糙的，不文雅的</td><td>unpolished manners<br>不文雅的举止</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His writing is unpolished but honest and often very funny.  他的文字粗糙，却真诚而常常很好笑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 unrefined</td><td>/ˌʌnrɪˈfaɪnd/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>粗俗的，未提炼的</td><td>unrefined manners<br>粗俗的举止</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His unrefined manners embarrassed the whole table.  他粗俗的举止让整桌人尴尬。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 vulgar</td><td>/ˈvʌlɡər/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>粗俗的，无教养的</td><td>vulgar display of wealth<br>粗俗的炫富</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Their vulgar display of wealth embarrassed even their closest friends.  他们粗俗的炫富让连最亲近的朋友都感到难堪。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 civilized</td><td>/ˈsɪvəˌlaɪzd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>文明的，有教养的</td><td>civilized behavior<br>文明的举止</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They settled the dispute in a calm, civilized manner over tea.  他们喝茶时以平静文明的方式解决了争端。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 cultivated</td><td>/ˈkʌltɪveɪtɪd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>有教养的，有修养的</td><td>a cultivated taste in art<br>高雅的艺术品位</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her cultivated manners impressed everyone at the dinner.  她优雅的举止让晚宴上的每个人都印象深刻。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 cultured</td><td>/ˈkʌltʃərd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>有教养的，有修养的</td><td>a cultured gentleman<br>有教养的绅士</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He was a cultured man who read poetry in four languages.  他是个有教养的人，能读四种语言的诗。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 genteel</td><td>/dʒenˈtiːl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>有教养的，不粗俗的</td><td>genteel manners<br>有教养的举止</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her genteel accent betrayed an expensive and very traditional upbringing.  她文雅的口音透露出昂贵而传统的成长背景。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 polished</td><td>/ˈpɑːlɪʃt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>有教养的</td><td>polished manners<br>优雅得体的举止</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His polished manners impressed everyone at the formal dinner.  他优雅得体的举止让正式晚宴上的每个人都印象深刻。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 refined</td><td>/rəˈfaɪnd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>精炼的，纯净的；有教养的</td><td>refined sugar<br>精制糖</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Refined sugar has been stripped of every impurity.  精制糖已去除了所有杂质。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 ultrarefined</del></td><td>/ˌʌltrərɪˈfaɪnd/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>极其考究的，过分精致的</td><td>ultrarefined taste<br>极其考究的品味</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The critic's ultrarefined taste made him hate every popular film.  这位评论家极其考究的品味让他讨厌每部流行电影。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 well-bred</td><td>/wɛl ˈbrɛd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>有教养的，有礼貌的</td><td>a well-bred young woman<br>一位有教养的年轻女子</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The well-bred guest thanked the host before leaving.  这位有教养的客人离开前向主人道了谢。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>recapitulate</b></td><td>/ˌriːkəˈpɪtʃuleɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>★</td><td>动词</td><td>概括，摘要</td><td>recapitulate the main points<br>概括要点</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Let me recapitulate the main points before we vote.  投票前让我概括一下要点。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 abstract</td><td>/æbˈstrækt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>做总结，概括；使分心</td><td>abstract a long report<br>概括一份长报告</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She abstracted the key findings into a single page.  她把主要发现概括成一页。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 outline</td><td>/ˈaʊtˌlaɪn/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>概要，大纲；概述</td><td>a rough outline<br>粗略的大纲</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He sketched an outline of the project in ten minutes.  他十分钟内勾出了项目的大纲。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 summarize</td><td>/ˈsʌməraɪz/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>动词</td><td>总结，概述</td><td>summarize the argument<br>概述论点</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Please summarize the report in three sentences for the board.  请把报告用三句话概述给董事会。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 synopsize</del></td><td>/sɪˈnɑpsaɪz/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>概述，做提要</td><td>synopsize a novel<br>概述一部小说</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Can you synopsize the plot in two sentences?  你能用两句话概述剧情吗？</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>rage</b></td><td>/reɪdʒ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>暴怒</td><td>fly into a rage<br>勃然大怒</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He flew into a rage when the waiter spilled the soup.  服务员把汤打翻时，他勃然大怒。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 agitation</td><td>/ˌædʒəˈteɪʃən/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>焦虑不安，激动</td><td>in a state of agitation<br>处于焦躁不安状态</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He paced the hallway in a state of visible agitation.  他在走廊里踱步，显得焦躁不安。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 deliriousness</del></td><td>/dɪˈlɪriəsnəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>神志昏乱；极度兴奋</td><td>the deliriousness of fever<br>高烧引起的昏乱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 In his deliriousness he mistook the nurse for his sister.  昏乱中他把护士当成了自己的姐姐。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 delirium</td><td>/dɪˈlɪriəm/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>精神错乱；极度兴奋，发狂</td><td>in a feverish delirium<br>处于发烧的昏乱中</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 For two nights the fever kept him in a restless delirium.  高烧使他两夜都处在不安的精神错乱之中。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 distraction</td><td>/dɪsˈtrækʃən/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>心烦意乱，精神错乱</td><td>drive him to distraction<br>使他心烦意乱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The constant noise drove the exhausted writer to distraction.  持续的噪音让疲惫的作家心烦意乱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 furor</td><td>/ˈfjʊrɔːr/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>喧闹，狂怒；激动，狂热</td><td>cause a public furor<br>引起公众哗然</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The painting caused a public furor when it was first shown.  这幅画首次展出时引起公众哗然。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 hysteria</td><td>/hɪˈsteriə/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>歇斯底里，狂热</td><td>mass hysteria in the crowd<br>人群中的集体狂热</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Rumors of invasion produced mass hysteria across the small town.  入侵的谣言在小镇引发集体歇斯底里。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 rampage</td><td>/ˈræmˌpeɪdʒ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>横冲直撞，暴怒乱闯</td><td>go on a rampage<br>大肆撒野闹事</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Elephants rampaged through the village, destroying several huts.  大象横冲直撞穿过村子，毁了几间棚屋。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>ragged</b></td><td>/ˈræɡɪd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>凹凸不平的，不光滑的</td><td>a ragged edge<br>参差不齐的边缘</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The saw left a ragged edge along the whole length of the board.  锯子在整块木板上留下参差不平的边缘。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 broken</td><td>/ˈbroʊkən/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>高低不平的；破损的</td><td>broken ground<br>高低不平的地面</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The horses slowed across the broken ground near the ravine.  马匹在峡谷附近高低不平的地面上放慢了脚步。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 bumpy</td><td>/ˈbʌmpi/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>坑洼不平的</td><td>a bumpy road<br>坑洼的道路</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The bumpy track shook every bolt loose on the old truck.  坑洼的土路把老卡车上每颗螺栓都震松了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 coarse</td><td>/kɔːrs/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>粗糙的，表面不平整的；粗俗的</td><td>coarse language<br>粗俗的言语</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The workers used coarse language that shocked the visiting students.  工人们说的粗话让来访的学生们大吃一惊。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 irregular</td><td>/ɪˈreɡjələr/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不规则的，不平整的</td><td>an irregular shape<br>不规则的形状</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The path was made of irregular stones of many sizes.  这条小路由各种大小不规则的石头铺成。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 jagged</td><td>/ˈdʒæɡəd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>锯齿状的，参差不齐的</td><td>jagged rocks<br>锯齿状的岩石</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Jagged rocks below the cliff made landing impossible.  崖下锯齿状的岩石使登陆无法进行。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 lumpy</td><td>/ˈlʌmpi/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>多疙块的，不平整的</td><td>a lumpy mattress<br>凹凸不平的床垫</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 I slept badly on a lumpy mattress in the attic room.  我在阁楼那张凹凸不平的床垫上没睡好。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 pebbly</td><td>/ˈpɛbli/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>多卵石的，粗粒的</td><td>a pebbly beach<br>卵石遍布的海滩</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The pebbly beach hurt our feet but the water was clear.  卵石海滩硌脚，但水很清。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 rough</td><td>/ˈrʌf/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>粗糙的，粗暴的；艰苦的</td><td>a rough surface<br>粗糙的表面</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The road grew rough as we climbed into the hills.  我们爬上山时路变得坎坷不平。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 roughened</td><td>/ˈrʌfənd/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>变粗糙的</td><td>roughened hands<br>变粗糙的双手</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Years of rope work left his palms roughened and hard.  多年拉绳的活计让他手掌变得粗糙坚硬。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 rugged</td><td>/ˈrʌɡəd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>坚毅的；粗糙不平的</td><td>rugged terrain<br>险峻的地形</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Only rugged travelers attempt that trail in the winter.  只有意志坚毅的旅行者才会在冬天走那条小径。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 scraggy</td><td>/ˈskræɡi/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>粗糙不平的；瘦骨嶙峋的</td><td>scraggy cliffs<br>嶙峋的峭壁</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Goats climbed the scraggy slope without any trouble.  山羊毫不费力地爬上那道嶙峋的坡。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 even</td><td>/ˈiːvən/</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>平坦的，均匀的</td><td>an even surface<br>平坦的表面</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Sand the board until the surface feels perfectly even.  把板子打磨到表面完全平整。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 flat</td><td>/ˈflæt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>单调无光的，乏味的</td><td>a flat performance<br>平淡无味的表演</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The paint dried to a flat, lifeless gray.  油漆干成了一种无光而死板的灰色。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 level</td><td>/ˈlevəl/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>水平的，平的</td><td>on level ground<br>在平地上</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Pitch the tent on level ground away from the stream.  把帐篷搭在远离溪水的平地上。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 plane</td><td>/ˈpleɪn/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>平坦的；平面</td><td>a plane surface<br>平整的表面</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Grinding produced a perfectly plane surface for the lens.  研磨为这枚镜片做出了完全平整的表面。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>rail</b></td><td>/reɪl/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>痛斥，抱怨</td><td>rail against injustice<br>猛烈抨击不公</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Students railed against the unfair new grading policy all week.  学生们整周都在痛斥不公平的新评分政策。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 baste</td><td>/ˈbeɪst/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>痛打；严厉批评</td><td>baste him with criticism<br>用批评痛击他</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The columnist basted the mayor for wasting so much public money.  专栏作家痛批市长浪费了大量公款。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 berate</td><td>/bɪˈreɪt/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>严厉指责，斥责</td><td>berate him publicly<br>公开斥责他</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The coach began to berate his players in front of reporters.  教练开始在记者面前严厉斥责他的球员。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 castigate</td><td>/ˈkæstɪɡeɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>强烈（公开）指责</td><td>castigate for negligence<br>因疏忽而受斥责</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The judge castigated the company for its negligence.  法官严厉指责该公司的疏忽。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 chastise</td><td>/tʃæˈstaɪz/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>严厉批评，责骂</td><td>chastise for laziness<br>因懒惰而受责骂</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The coach chastised the players for their careless passing.  教练严厉批评球员们传球草率。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 hammer</td><td>/ˈhæmər/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>屡次重申</td><td>hammer home the point<br>反复强调这一点</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The coach hammered home the importance of defense at every single practice.  教练在每一次训练中都反复强调防守的重要性。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 lambaste</td><td>/læmˈbeɪst/</td><td>☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>严厉斥责，痛骂</td><td>lambaste the government<br>痛斥政府</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Critics lambasted the film for its shallow, sentimental ending.  批评家严厉斥责这部电影结局浅薄而煽情。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 rebuke</td><td>/rɪˈbjuːk/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>斥责，责难</td><td>a sharp rebuke<br>严厉的斥责</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The judge rebuked the lawyer for interrupting the witness.  法官斥责律师打断证人发言。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 reprimand</td><td>/ˈreprəˌmænd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>训斥，正式谴责</td><td>reprimand an employee<br>训斥员工</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The officer was reprimanded for leaving his post early.  这名警官因提早离岗而受到训斥。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 reproach</td><td>/rɪˈproʊtʃ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>批评，责备</td><td>reproach oneself for<br>因某事自责</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She reproached him for never returning her calls.  她责备他从不回她的电话。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 upbraid</del></td><td>/ʌpˈbreɪd/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>（严厉地）谴责，责骂</td><td>upbraid him for lying<br>因撒谎而责骂他</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The manager upbraided him for missing three deadlines in one month.  经理因他一个月内三次误期而严厉责骂他。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 accredit</td><td>/əˈkredɪt/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>认可，授权；归功于</td><td>accredit the university<br>认可该大学资质</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The board finally accredited the new nursing program.  委员会终于认可了这个新的护理专业。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 applaud</td><td>/əˈplɔːd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>称赞，赞成；鼓掌</td><td>applaud the decision<br>赞成这一决定</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Teachers applauded the decision to reduce homework for young pupils.  老师们赞成给低年级学生减少作业的决定。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 commend</td><td>/kəˈmend/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>赞扬，表扬</td><td>commend her courage<br>赞扬她的勇气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The principal commended her courage in front of the whole school.  校长在全校面前赞扬了她的勇气。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 eulogize</td><td>/ˈjuːlədʒaɪz/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>称赞；颂扬</td><td>eulogize the late leader<br>颂扬已故领袖</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Colleagues gathered to eulogize the scientist who had trained them all.  同事们聚在一起颂扬这位培养了他们所有人的科学家。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 extol</td><td>/ɪkˈstoʊl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>赞美；吹捧</td><td>extol his virtues<br>赞美他的品德</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Neighbors still extol his kindness during the long winter flood.  邻居们至今仍赞美他在漫长冬季洪水期间的善举。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 laud</td><td>/ˈlɔːd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>赞美，称赞</td><td>laud his courage<br>赞扬他的勇气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Critics lauded the young pianist for her fearless interpretation.  评论家赞扬这位年轻钢琴家大胆的演绎。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 praise</td><td>/ˈpreɪz/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>赞扬，称赞</td><td>win high praise<br>获得高度赞扬</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her first novel won high praise from serious critics.  她的第一部小说赢得了严肃评论家的高度赞扬。</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

## Unit 6

<table>
<thead><tr>
<th>单词</th>
<th>音标</th>
<th>常用</th>
<th>口语</th>
<th>词性</th>
<th>中文释义</th>
<th>常用短语</th>
</tr></thead>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>rakish</b></td><td>/ˈreɪkɪʃ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>放荡的，行为不检点的</td><td>a rakish reputation<br>放荡不检的名声</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His rakish reputation kept respectable families from inviting him to dinner.  他放荡不检的名声使正派家庭不愿请他吃饭。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 debased</td><td>/dəˈbeɪst/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>堕落的，贬损的</td><td>debased taste<br>低劣的品味</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He complained about the debased state of public debate.  他抱怨公共辩论的堕落状态。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 debauched</td><td>/dɪˈbɔtʃt/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>放荡的，堕落的</td><td>a debauched lifestyle<br>放荡的生活方式</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The novel follows a debauched young lord toward his ruin.  小说讲一位放荡的年轻贵族走向毁灭。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 demoralized</td><td>/dɪˈmɔːrəˌlaɪzd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>堕落的，道德败坏的；士气低落的</td><td>a demoralized court<br>堕落的宫廷</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The chronicle describes a demoralized court given over to gambling and drink.  编年史描述了一个沉溺于赌博与饮酒的堕落宫廷。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 depraved</td><td>/diˈpreɪvd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>堕落的，道德败坏的</td><td>a depraved appetite<br>堕落的嗜好</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Critics called the film depraved, which only sold more tickets.  批评者称这部电影堕落，结果票卖得更多。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dissolute</td><td>/ˈdɪsəluːt/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>放荡的，无节制的</td><td>a dissolute lifestyle<br>放荡的生活方式</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The novel follows a dissolute lifestyle of late nights and squandered fortunes.  小说描写了通宵纵乐、挥霍家财的放荡生活方式。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 libertine</td><td>/ˈlɪbərtiːn/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>放荡不羁者</td><td>an unrepentant libertine<br>不知悔改的放荡者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The play portrays an unrepentant libertine who ruins everyone around him.  这部戏刻画了一个毁掉身边所有人的不知悔改的放荡者。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 perverse</td><td>/pərˈvɜːrs/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>反常固执的，任性的</td><td>take perverse pleasure<br>反常地感到快意</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He takes perverse pleasure in arguing against every popular opinion.  他反常地乐于反驳每一个流行观点。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 perverted</td><td>/pərˈvɜːrtɪd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>扭曲的，反常堕落的</td><td>a perverted sense of justice<br>扭曲的正义感</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He justified the theft with a perverted sense of fairness.  他用一种扭曲的公平感为盗窃辩解。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 reprobate</td><td>/ˈreprəbeɪt/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>堕落者，道德败坏的人</td><td>an old reprobate<br>一个老无赖</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The novel's hero is a charming reprobate with no money.  小说的主角是个迷人却身无分文的堕落者。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 good</td><td>/ˈɡʊd/</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>有利的，好的</td><td>a good opportunity<br>有利的机会</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 This is a good moment to renegotiate the contract.  这是重新谈判合同的有利时机。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 moral</td><td>/ˈmɔːrəl/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>道德的；道德准则</td><td>a moral obligation<br>道义上的义务</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They felt a moral obligation to shelter the refugees.  他们觉得有道义上的义务收留难民。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 pure</td><td>/ˈpjʊr/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>纯粹的，完全的</td><td>pure coincidence<br>纯属巧合</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Meeting her there was pure coincidence, nothing more.  在那里遇见她纯属巧合，别无其他。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 righteous</td><td>/ˈraɪtʃəs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>正直的，正义的</td><td>righteous indignation<br>义愤</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her righteous indignation at the ruling filled the courtroom.  她对判决的义愤充满法庭。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 virtuous</td><td>/ˈvɜːrtʃuəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>品德高尚的，正直的</td><td>a virtuous life<br>高尚的生活</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He led a quiet, virtuous life and helped his neighbors freely.  他过着平静高尚的生活，乐于帮助邻里。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>ramble</b></td><td>/ˈræmbl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>不及物动词、名词</td><td>漫谈，东拉西扯；漫步</td><td>ramble on about<br>喋喋不休地扯</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He rambled on about his childhood for a full hour.  他絮絮叨叨讲了整整一小时童年往事。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 blather</td><td>/ˈblæðər/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>唠唠叨叨地说，胡扯</td><td>blather on about nothing<br>东拉西扯地唠</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He blathered on about traffic while everyone waited to order.  大家等着点菜，他还在唠唠叨叨说交通。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 chat</td><td>/ˈtʃæt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>闲聊，聊天</td><td>chat over coffee<br>喝咖啡时闲聊</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They chatted over coffee for nearly two pleasant hours.  他们喝着咖啡愉快地闲聊了近两个小时。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 chatter</td><td>/ˈtʃætər/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>喋喋不休，闲扯</td><td>chatter endlessly<br>喋喋不休地说</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The children chattered endlessly during the long bus ride.  孩子们在漫长的巴士行程中喋喋不休。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 drivel</td><td>/ˈdrɪvl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>胡言乱语</td><td>talk complete drivel<br>满口胡言</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The pamphlet talked complete drivel about curing serious illness with colored light.  那本小册子满口胡言，说用彩色光能治重病。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 maunder</td><td>/ˈmɔndər/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>动词</td><td>漫无目的地说；闲逛</td><td>maunder on for hours<br>唠叨好几个小时</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old sailor maundered on about ports nobody had heard of.  老水手漫无边际地讲着没人听过的港口。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 patter</td><td>/ˈpætər/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>喋喋不休地说；急促轻响</td><td>the salesman's patter<br>推销员的顺口话</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The magician kept up a cheerful patter while shuffling the cards.  魔术师一边洗牌一边说着轻快的顺口话。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 prattle</td><td>/ˈprætəl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>闲扯，唠叨</td><td>prattle on for hours<br>唠叨好几个小时</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She prattled happily about her garden while we drank our tea.  我们喝茶时她开心地闲扯她的花园。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>rambunctious</b></td><td>/ræmˈbʌŋkʃəs/</td><td>★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>喧闹失控的，撒欢的</td><td>rambunctious children<br>闹得没边的孩子</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Three rambunctious puppies knocked over the coffee table.  三只闹腾的小狗把茶几撞翻了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 boisterous</td><td>/ˈbɔɪstərəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>喧嚷的，吵闹的</td><td>a boisterous crowd<br>喧闹的人群</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A boisterous crowd filled the square outside the stadium.  一群喧闹的人挤满了体育场外的广场。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 clamorous</td><td>/ˈklæmərəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>喧闹的；大声要求的</td><td>clamorous demands for reform<br>高声要求改革</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The clamorous market made quiet conversation impossible.  喧闹的市场让人根本无法安静交谈。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 raucous</td><td>/ˈrɔːkəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>刺耳的，喧闹的</td><td>raucous laughter<br>刺耳的大笑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Raucous laughter spilled out of the crowded pub.  刺耳的笑声从挤满人的酒吧里传出来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 riotous</td><td>/ˈraɪətəs/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>繁茂的；喧闹的</td><td>a riotous display of color<br>缤纷繁茂的色彩</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The garden was a riotous mass of summer flowers.  花园里夏花繁茂缤纷。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 rowdy</td><td>/ˈraʊdi/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>吵闹的，撒野的</td><td>a rowdy party<br>闹翻天的派对</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The rowdy fans kept the whole neighborhood awake.  吵闹的球迷让整个街区都睡不着。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 tumultuous</td><td>/tuˈmʌltʃuəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>喧闹的；动乱的</td><td>tumultuous applause<br>雷鸣般的掌声</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The pianist returned to tumultuous applause from the standing crowd.  钢琴家返场时全场起立，掌声雷动。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 turbulent</td><td>/ˈtɜːrbjələnt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>动荡的</td><td>a turbulent period<br>动荡的时期</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They married during a turbulent period of war and economic collapse.  他们在战争与经济崩溃的动荡时期结了婚。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 orderly</td><td>/ˈɔːrdərli/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>有秩序的，整齐的</td><td>an orderly queue<br>整齐的队伍</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The students left the hall in an orderly line.  学生们排成整齐的队伍离开礼堂。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>ramshackle</b></td><td>/ˈræmʃækl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>摇摇欲坠的</td><td>a ramshackle barn<br>摇摇欲坠的谷仓</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They stored hay in a ramshackle barn that leaned toward the road.  他们把干草存放在一座向马路倾斜的摇摇欲坠的谷仓里。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 rickety</td><td>/ˈrɪkəti/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不稳的</td><td>a rickety wooden chair<br>摇摇晃晃的木椅</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 We climbed a rickety wooden staircase to reach the attic bedroom.  我们爬上摇摇晃晃的木楼梯，来到阁楼卧室。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 shaky</td><td>/ˈʃeɪki/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不可靠的，不稳的</td><td>shaky reasoning<br>站不住脚的推理</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The theory rests on shaky historical evidence.  该理论建立在不可靠的历史证据上。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 tottering</td><td>/ˈtɑːtərɪŋ/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>摇摇晃晃的，不稳的</td><td>a tottering fence<br>摇晃的栅栏</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A tottering stack of books finally slid onto the floor.  一摞摇晃的书终于滑到了地上。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 concrete</td><td>/ˈkɑːŋkriːt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>具体的，实在的；明确的</td><td>concrete evidence<br>确凿的证据</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The detective needed concrete evidence rather than clever theories.  侦探需要确凿的证据，而不是巧妙的推论。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 firm</td><td>/ˈfɜːrm/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>坚定的，坚固的</td><td>a firm decision<br>坚定的决定</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She remained firm despite enormous pressure from the board.  尽管董事会施加巨大压力，她依然坚定。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 solid</td><td>/ˈsɑːlɪd/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>粗壮结实的</td><td>a solid, heavy build<br>粗壮结实的体格</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The wrestler had a solid, heavy build unlike his slender opponent.  这名摔跤手体格粗壮结实，与瘦削的对手截然不同。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 sturdy</td><td>/ˈstɜːrdi/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>强健的，结实的</td><td>a sturdy wooden table<br>结实的木桌</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A sturdy wooden table stood in the middle of the farmhouse kitchen.  农舍厨房中间摆着一张结实的木桌。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>rancor</b></td><td>/ˈræŋkər/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>敌意，深仇</td><td>speak without rancor<br>不带怨恨地说</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She described the long dispute without any rancor toward her opponents.  她讲述这场长期争端时对对手毫无怨恨。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 animosity</td><td>/ˌænəˈmɑːsəti/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>敌意，憎恶</td><td>deep animosity between them<br>他们之间的深仇</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Years of competition bred real animosity between the two families.  多年的竞争在两家之间催生了真正的敌意。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 animus</td><td>/ˈænɪməs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>敌意</td><td>personal animus against him<br>对他的个人敌意</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His criticism seemed driven by personal animus rather than honest doubt.  他的批评似乎出于个人敌意，而非真诚的质疑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 antagonism</td><td>/ænˈtæɡənɪzəm/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>敌对，对抗</td><td>open antagonism toward reform<br>对改革的公开敌视</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 There was open antagonism between the manager and his staff.  经理与员工之间存在公开的敌对。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 antipathy</td><td>/ænˈtɪpəθi/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>厌恶，反感</td><td>mutual antipathy<br>相互反感</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 There has long been an antipathy between the two departments.  这两个部门之间长期存在反感。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 bitterness</td><td>/ˈbɪtərnəs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>怨恨；苦味</td><td>lingering bitterness<br>挥之不去的怨恨</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Old bitterness surfaced whenever the two brothers discussed money.  两兄弟一谈钱，旧怨就浮上来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 gall</td><td>/ɡɔːl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>（使）焦躁，激怒</td><td>galled by the remark<br>被那句话惹恼</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His smug tone galled everyone sitting around the conference table.  他自鸣得意的语气激怒了围坐在会议桌旁的每个人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 grudge</td><td>/ɡrʌdʒ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>怨恨，仇恨</td><td>hold a grudge<br>心怀怨恨</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She held a grudge against her cousin for more than ten years.  她对表姐怀恨在心十多年。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 hostility</td><td>/hɑːˈstɪləti/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>敌意，敌对</td><td>open hostility toward<br>对…公开的敌意</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The plan met with open hostility from local residents.  该计划遭到当地居民的公开敌视。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 jaundice</td><td>/ˈdʒɔːndɪs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>（因嫉妒或厌世而产生的）偏见</td><td>view with jaundice<br>带偏见看待</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Long unemployment had filled him with jaundice toward everyone still working.  长期失业使他对所有还有工作的人心生偏见。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 amity</td><td>/ˈæməti/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>友好关系，亲善和睦</td><td>a treaty of amity<br>友好条约</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The two nations signed a treaty of amity and commerce.  两国签署了友好通商条约。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 harmony</td><td>/ˈhɑːrməni/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>和谐，融洽</td><td>live in harmony with<br>与…和谐共处</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The two communities have lived in harmony for generations.  这两个社区世代和睦相处。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 goodwill</td><td>/ˈɡʊdwɪl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>善意，友好</td><td>a gesture of goodwill<br>善意的表示</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They returned the boat as a gesture of goodwill.  他们归还了那条船，以示善意。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 rapport</td><td>/ræˈpɔːr/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>和睦，友好</td><td>build a good rapport<br>建立良好关系</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The new teacher quickly built a good rapport with her difficult class.  这位新老师很快与她难带的班级建立了友好关系。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 rapprochement</td><td>/ˌræproʊʃˈmɑːn/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>和解，恢复友好</td><td>a slow rapprochement<br>缓慢的和解</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Trade talks began a cautious rapprochement between the two capitals.  贸易谈判开启了两国首都间谨慎的和解。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>random</b></td><td>/ˈrændəm/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>随机的，随意的</td><td>a random sample<br>随机样本</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The survey drew a random sample of two thousand households nationwide.  这项调查在全国抽取了两千户家庭的随机样本。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 methodical</td><td>/məˈθɑːdɪkl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>井然有序的，有条理的</td><td>a methodical approach<br>有条理的方法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A methodical search of the archive finally produced the missing land records.  对档案的有条理搜查终于找出了缺失的土地记录。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 orderly</td><td>/ˈɔːrdərli/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>有秩序的，整齐的</td><td>an orderly queue<br>整齐的队伍</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The students left the hall in an orderly line.  学生们排成整齐的队伍离开礼堂。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 organized</td><td>/ˈɔːrɡəˌnaɪzd/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>有条理的，有组织的</td><td>highly organized files<br>条理分明的文件</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She keeps an organized notebook for every course she takes.  她为每门课都备有一本条理分明的笔记。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 regular</td><td>/ˈreɡjələr/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>规律的，通常的</td><td>on a regular basis<br>定期地</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He goes swimming on a regular basis every week.  他每周都定期去游泳。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 systematic</td><td>/ˌsɪstəˈmætɪk/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>系统的，有条理的</td><td>a systematic review<br>系统性回顾</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The team took a systematic approach to checking each component.  团队用系统的方法逐一检查了每个部件。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 systematized</td><td>/ˈsɪstəmətaɪzd/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>系统化的，有条理的</td><td>a systematized filing method<br>系统化的归档方法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her notes were carefully systematized by subject and by date.  她的笔记按主题和日期作了系统整理。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>rankle</b></td><td>/ˈræŋkl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>激怒</td><td>rankle in his mind<br>在他心里作痛</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The unfair remark continued to rankle him for many years afterward.  那句不公平的评论此后多年一直令他愤懑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 aggravate</td><td>/ˈæɡrəveɪt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>加重，恶化；激怒</td><td>aggravate the situation<br>使局势恶化</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Running on the injury will only aggravate it.  带着伤跑步只会让伤势加重。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 enrage</td><td>/enˈreɪdʒ/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使愤怒，激怒</td><td>enrage the audience<br>激怒观众</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His flippant apology only enraged the families further.  他轻率的道歉只让那些家庭更加愤怒。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 exasperate</td><td>/ɪɡˈzæspəreɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使恼怒，激怒</td><td>exasperate his parents<br>使他的父母恼火</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His constant lateness exasperated even the most patient teachers.  他总是迟到，连最有耐心的老师都被激怒了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 incense</td><td>/ɪnˈsens/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>激怒</td><td>incense the public<br>激怒公众</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The proposal is certain to incense feminists across the country.  这项提案肯定会激怒全国的女权主义者。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 inflame</td><td>/ɪnˈfleɪm/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使加剧</td><td>inflame public anger<br>使公众愤怒加剧</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The mayor's careless comments only inflamed public anger over the housing shortage.  市长轻率的言论只是加剧了公众对住房短缺的愤怒。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 infuriate</td><td>/ɪnˈfjʊrieɪt/</td><td>★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>激怒</td><td>infuriate the critics<br>激怒批评者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The decision to cut pensions infuriated thousands of workers across the country.  削减养老金的决定激怒了全国成千上万的工人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 ire</td><td>/ˈaɪr/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>愤怒，怒火</td><td>draw the ire of critics<br>引来批评者的怒火</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The new tax drew the ire of small business owners.  新税收招致了小企业主的愤怒。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 madden</td><td>/ˈmædən/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使发狂，使愤怒</td><td>madden the audience<br>使观众暴怒</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The endless delays maddened passengers waiting at the gate.  无休止的延误让在登机口等待的乘客怒不可遏。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 rile</td><td>/raɪl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>刺激，惹怒</td><td>rile up the crowd<br>激怒人群</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Nothing riles him more than being interrupted in the middle of a sentence.  没有什么比话说到一半被打断更让他恼火。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 roil</td><td>/rɔɪl/</td><td>☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>激怒</td><td>roil the crowd<br>激怒人群</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The careless remarks of the mayor roiled the crowd outside the courthouse.  市长轻率的言论激怒了法院外的人群。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 delight</td><td>/dɪˈlaɪt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>使高兴，使愉快；愉悦</td><td>delight the audience<br>使观众高兴</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The young pianist delighted the audience with a lively encore.  这位年轻钢琴家用一首活泼的加演曲取悦了观众。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 gratify</td><td>/ˈɡrætɪfaɪ/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使满足，使高兴</td><td>gratify a desire<br>满足某种欲望</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It gratified her to see her students succeed.  看到学生成功让她很欣慰。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 please</td><td>/ˈpliːz/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>及物动词、不及物动词</td><td>使高兴，使满意</td><td>please the crowd<br>取悦人群</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The simple ending pleased readers far more than critics expected.  这个简单的结局比评论家预料的更让读者满意。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>rant</b></td><td>/rænt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>（尤指长时间的）训斥，责骂</td><td>go on a rant<br>大发一通牢骚</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The coach went on a long rant about the team's careless defending.  教练就球队漫不经心的防守训斥了很久。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 castigation</del></td><td>/ˌkæstɪˈɡeɪʃən/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>严厉斥责</td><td>public castigation<br>公开斥责</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The report's castigation of the agency filled forty angry pages.  报告对该机构的严厉斥责写了四十页。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 diatribe</td><td>/ˈdaɪətraɪb/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>长篇抨击性演讲</td><td>launch into a diatribe<br>开始长篇抨击</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His speech turned into a long diatribe against modern popular music.  他的演讲变成了对现代流行音乐的长篇抨击。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 harangue</td><td>/həˈræŋ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>（发表）长篇大论</td><td>a long harangue<br>长篇的训话</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The manager harangued his staff for twenty minutes about missed deadlines.  经理就错过截止日期的事对员工发表了二十分钟的长篇训话。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 lambasting</td><td>/læmˈbeɪstɪŋ/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>痛斥，严厉批评</td><td>a public lambasting<br>公开的痛斥</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He took a lambasting from the press after the failed launch.  发射失败后他受到媒体一顿痛斥。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 philippic</del></td><td>/fɪˈlɪpɪk/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>激烈的抨击演说</td><td>deliver a fierce philippic<br>发表激烈的抨击演说</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The senator's philippic against the bill lasted two full hours.  这位参议员抨击该法案的演说整整两小时。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 reprimand</td><td>/ˈreprəˌmænd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>训斥，正式谴责</td><td>reprimand an employee<br>训斥员工</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The officer was reprimanded for leaving his post early.  这名警官因提早离岗而受到训斥。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 reproach</td><td>/rɪˈproʊtʃ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>批评，责备</td><td>reproach oneself for<br>因某事自责</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She reproached him for never returning her calls.  她责备他从不回她的电话。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 vituperation</td><td>/vaɪˌtupəˈreɪʃən/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>谩骂，苛责</td><td>a torrent of vituperation<br>一阵谩骂</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She endured a torrent of vituperation without replying once.  她忍受一阵谩骂而一句未回。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 encomium</td><td>/enˈkoʊmiəm/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>赞颂之词</td><td>deliver a warm encomium<br>致以热情的赞颂</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The mayor delivered a warm encomium at the retiring teacher's farewell dinner.  市长在退休教师的告别晚宴上致以热情的赞颂。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 eulogy</td><td>/ˈjuːlədʒi/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>悼词；颂词，赞辞</td><td>deliver a moving eulogy<br>致感人的悼词</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She delivered a moving eulogy at her grandfather's funeral.  她在祖父的葬礼上致了感人的悼词。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 panegyric</td><td>/ˌpænəˈdʒɪrɪk/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>赞颂之词，颂文</td><td>deliver a long panegyric<br>发表一篇长颂词</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The retirement dinner ended with a long panegyric to the old professor.  退休晚宴以对这位老教授的长篇颂词结束。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 rhapsody</td><td>/ˈræpsədi/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>狂喜，热情赞美；狂想曲</td><td>go into rhapsodies<br>大加赞美</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He went into rhapsodies about the noodles he ate in Osaka.  他对在大阪吃的面条大加赞美。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 tribute</td><td>/ˈtrɪbjuːt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>称颂，颂词</td><td>pay tribute to her work<br>对她的工作表示称颂</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Colleagues paid warm tribute to her forty years of patient teaching.  同事们对她四十年的耐心教学致以热情的颂扬。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>rapacious</b></td><td>/rəˈpeɪʃəs/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>过度贪婪的</td><td>rapacious business practices<br>贪婪的商业行径</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Rapacious business practices drove several small competitors out of the market.  贪婪的商业行径把几家小竞争者挤出了市场。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 acquisitive</td><td>/əˈkwɪzətɪv/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>贪婪的</td><td>an acquisitive society<br>物欲横流的社会</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The acquisitive collector bought every painting that came onto the market.  这位贪得无厌的收藏家买下了市面上出现的每一幅画。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 avaricious</td><td>/ˌævəˈrɪʃəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>贪财的，贪婪的</td><td>an avaricious landlord<br>贪财的房东</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The avaricious banker cared for nothing but the size of his bonus.  这个贪财的银行家只在乎奖金的多少。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 avid</td><td>/ˈævɪd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>急切渴望的，热衷的</td><td>an avid reader<br>热衷阅读的人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 My grandmother is an avid gardener even in winter.  我祖母即使在冬天也是个热衷园艺的人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 coveting</td><td>/ˈkʌvɪtɪŋ/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词、形容词</td><td>贪求，垂涎</td><td>coveting a neighbor's wealth<br>垂涎邻人的财富</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Endless coveting of other people's houses made him miserable.  不停垂涎别人的房子让他很痛苦。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 covetous</td><td>/ˈkʌvətəs/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>贪婪的，渴求财富的</td><td>covetous of his wealth<br>贪求他的财富</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He cast a covetous eye on his neighbor's newly bought tractor.  他贪婪地盯着邻居新买的拖拉机。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 grasping</td><td>/ˈɡræspɪŋ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>贪婪的，唯利是图的</td><td>a grasping moneylender<br>唯利是图的放贷人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His grasping relatives fought over the estate before the funeral ended.  他贪婪的亲戚在葬礼结束前就争夺遗产。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 mercenary</td><td>/ˈmɜːrsəneri/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>唯利是图的，贪婪的</td><td>mercenary motives<br>唯利是图的动机</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her mercenary motives became obvious once the inheritance was finally mentioned.  一提到遗产，她唯利是图的动机就显露无遗。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 moneygrubbing</del></td><td>/ˈmʌniɡrʌbɪŋ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>贪财的，唯利是图的</td><td>a moneygrubbing landlord<br>唯利是图的房东</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Neighbors called him a moneygrubbing landlord behind his back.  邻居们背后叫他唯利是图的房东。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 content</td><td>/ˈkɑːntent/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>内容，内涵；使满足</td><td>the content of the letter<br>信的内容</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The teacher praised the style but questioned the content of my essay.  老师赞赏我文章的文风，却质疑其内容。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 sated</td><td>/ˈseɪtɪd/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>满足的，饱足的</td><td>sated with food<br>吃得饱足</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Sated after the long dinner, nobody wanted dessert.  长长的晚餐后大家都饱了，没人要甜点。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 satiated</td><td>/ˈseɪʃieɪtɪd/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>饱足的，厌腻的</td><td>satiated with luxury<br>对奢侈已厌腻</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Readers satiated with sequels ignored the studio's newest film.  对续集已厌腻的观众忽略了片方的新片。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 satisfied</td><td>/ˈsætəsˌfaɪd/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>满意的，满足的</td><td>a satisfied customer<br>满意的顾客</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She looked satisfied with the results of the experiment.  她对实验结果看上去很满意。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>rapport</b></td><td>/ræˈpɔːr/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>和睦，友好</td><td>build a good rapport<br>建立良好关系</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The new teacher quickly built a good rapport with her difficult class.  这位新老师很快与她难带的班级建立了友好关系。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 amity</td><td>/ˈæməti/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>友好关系，亲善和睦</td><td>a treaty of amity<br>友好条约</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The two nations signed a treaty of amity and commerce.  两国签署了友好通商条约。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 communion</td><td>/kəmˈjuːnjən/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>亲密交流，心灵共鸣</td><td>quiet communion with nature<br>与自然的静默交流</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Long walks gave him a quiet communion with the hills.  长途步行让他与群山有了静默的交流。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 concord</td><td>/ˈkɑːŋkɔːrd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>一致，和睦</td><td>live in concord<br>和睦相处</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The treaty brought decades of concord between the two states.  该条约给两国带来数十年的和睦。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fellowship</td><td>/ˈfeloʊˌʃɪp/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>友情，团体；研究员职位</td><td>a fellowship of scholars<br>学者组成的团体</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The choir offered both music and warm fellowship every week.  合唱团每周既提供音乐也带来温暖情谊。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 harmony</td><td>/ˈhɑːrməni/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>和谐，融洽</td><td>live in harmony with<br>与…和谐共处</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The two communities have lived in harmony for generations.  这两个社区世代和睦相处。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 rapprochement</td><td>/ˌræproʊʃˈmɑːn/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>和解，恢复友好</td><td>a slow rapprochement<br>缓慢的和解</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Trade talks began a cautious rapprochement between the two capitals.  贸易谈判开启了两国首都间谨慎的和解。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 animosity</td><td>/ˌænəˈmɑːsəti/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>敌意，憎恶</td><td>deep animosity between them<br>他们之间的深仇</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Years of competition bred real animosity between the two families.  多年的竞争在两家之间催生了真正的敌意。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 antagonism</td><td>/ænˈtæɡənɪzəm/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>敌对，对抗</td><td>open antagonism toward reform<br>对改革的公开敌视</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 There was open antagonism between the manager and his staff.  经理与员工之间存在公开的敌对。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 antipathy</td><td>/ænˈtɪpəθi/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>厌恶，反感</td><td>mutual antipathy<br>相互反感</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 There has long been an antipathy between the two departments.  这两个部门之间长期存在反感。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 bitterness</td><td>/ˈbɪtərnəs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>怨恨；苦味</td><td>lingering bitterness<br>挥之不去的怨恨</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Old bitterness surfaced whenever the two brothers discussed money.  两兄弟一谈钱，旧怨就浮上来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 enmity</td><td>/ˈenməti/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>敌意，仇恨</td><td>long-standing enmity<br>积怨已久的仇恨</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Old enmity between the families still poisons village life.  两家的旧仇至今仍毒害着村里的生活。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 hostility</td><td>/hɑːˈstɪləti/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>敌意，敌对</td><td>open hostility toward<br>对…公开的敌意</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The plan met with open hostility from local residents.  该计划遭到当地居民的公开敌视。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 jaundice</td><td>/ˈdʒɔːndɪs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>（因嫉妒或厌世而产生的）偏见</td><td>view with jaundice<br>带偏见看待</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Long unemployment had filled him with jaundice toward everyone still working.  长期失业使他对所有还有工作的人心生偏见。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 rancor</td><td>/ˈræŋkər/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>敌意，深仇</td><td>speak without rancor<br>不带怨恨地说</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She described the long dispute without any rancor toward her opponents.  她讲述这场长期争端时对对手毫无怨恨。</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

<tr><td colspan="7">例 The two communities have lived in harmony for generations.  这两个社区世代和睦相处。</td></tr>
</tbody>

<tr><td colspan="7">例 The new teacher quickly built a good rapport with her difficult class.  这位新老师很快与她难带的班级建立了友好关系。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 animosity</td><td>/ˌænəˈmɑːsəti/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>敌意，憎恶</td><td>deep animosity between them<br>他们之间的深仇</td></tr>

<tr><td colspan="7">例 There has long been an antipathy between the two departments.  这两个部门之间长期存在反感。</td></tr>
</tbody>

<tr><td colspan="7">例 Old bitterness surfaced whenever the two brothers discussed money.  两兄弟一谈钱，旧怨就浮上来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 enmity</td><td>/ˈenməti/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>敌意，仇恨</td><td>long-standing enmity<br>积怨已久的仇恨</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Old enmity between the families still poisons village life.  两家的旧仇至今仍毒害着村里的生活。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 hostility</td><td>/hɑːˈstɪləti/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>敌意，敌对</td><td>open hostility toward<br>对…公开的敌意</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The plan met with open hostility from local residents.  该计划遭到当地居民的公开敌视。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 jaundice</td><td>/ˈdʒɔːndɪs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>（因嫉妒或厌世而产生的）偏见</td><td>view with jaundice<br>带偏见看待</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Long unemployment had filled him with jaundice toward everyone still working.  长期失业使他对所有还有工作的人心生偏见。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 rancor</td><td>/ˈræŋkər/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>敌意，深仇</td><td>speak without rancor<br>不带怨恨地说</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She described the long dispute without any rancor toward her opponents.  她讲述这场长期争端时对对手毫无怨恨。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>rapscallion</b></td><td>/ræpˈskæliən/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>流氓，无赖；小捣蛋鬼</td><td>a young rapscallion<br>一个年轻流氓</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 That little rapscallion stole apples from the neighbor's tree again.  那个小捣蛋鬼又偷了邻居树上的苹果。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 brute</td><td>/ˈbruːt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>残暴之人，野蛮人</td><td>a drunken brute<br>醉酒的野蛮之人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The judge called him a brute who terrorized his neighbors.  法官称他是恐吓邻居的野蛮之徒。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 devil</td><td>/ˈdevəl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>魔鬼，恶魔；坏家伙</td><td>a poor devil<br>可怜的家伙</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 In the old story the devil offers wealth for a soul.  在那个古老故事里，恶魔用财富换取灵魂。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 evildoer</td><td>/ˈiːvəlˌduːər/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>作恶者，恶人</td><td>punish the evildoer<br>惩罚作恶者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 In these old tales every evildoer is punished by the last page.  在这些老故事里，每个作恶者到末页都受罚。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fiend</td><td>/ˈfiːnd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>恶魔，残忍之人；狂热者</td><td>a cruel fiend<br>残忍的恶魔</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The newspapers described the killer as a heartless fiend.  报纸把这个凶手描述成毫无心肠的恶魔。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 knave</td><td>/ˈneɪv/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>无赖，恶棍</td><td>a lying knave<br>撒谎的无赖</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The play's villain is a smiling knave who betrays everyone.  剧中反派是个笑面无赖，谁都背叛。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 miscreant</td><td>/ˈmɪskriənt/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>恶棍，罪犯；邪恶的</td><td>punish the miscreants<br>惩罚这些恶棍</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The judge promised to punish the miscreants who had vandalized the village school.  法官承诺惩罚那些破坏村校的恶棍。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 rascal</td><td>/ˈræskəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>无赖，流氓；小淘气</td><td>a little rascal<br>小淘气鬼</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 That rascal took the last piece of cake again.  那个小淘气又拿走了最后一块蛋糕。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 reprobate</td><td>/ˈreprəbeɪt/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>堕落者，道德败坏的人</td><td>an old reprobate<br>一个老无赖</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The novel's hero is a charming reprobate with no money.  小说的主角是个迷人却身无分文的堕落者。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 rogue</td><td>/ˈroʊɡ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>无赖，流氓</td><td>a charming rogue<br>讨人喜欢的无赖</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Audiences forgive the hero because he is a charming rogue.  观众原谅这位主角，因为他是个迷人的无赖。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 savage</td><td>/ˈsævədʒ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词、及物动词</td><td>凶残的，野蛮的；猛烈攻击</td><td>a savage beating<br>野蛮的殴打</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Critics savaged the film for its careless treatment of history.  批评家猛烈批评该片对历史的草率处理。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 scamp</td><td>/skæmp/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>淘气鬼；无赖</td><td>a cheerful little scamp<br>快活的小淘气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 That scamp hid my glasses in the flour jar again.  那个小淘气又把我的眼镜藏进面粉罐里。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 varlet</td><td>/ˈvɑrlɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>恶棍，无赖（古语）</td><td>a scheming varlet<br>心计多端的恶棍</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old comedy calls the servant a lazy varlet.  这部老喜剧把那仆人称作懒惰的无赖。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 wretch</td><td>/ˈretʃ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>可鄙之人；可怜的人</td><td>an ungrateful wretch<br>不知感恩的可鄙之人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The poor wretch had slept outside for three freezing nights.  这可怜人在外冻睡了三个夜晚。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 saint</td><td>/ˈseɪnt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>圣人，品德高尚的人</td><td>the patience of a saint<br>圣人般的耐心</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Teaching that class demands the patience of a saint.  教那个班需要圣人般的耐心。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>rapt</b></td><td>/ræpt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>全神贯注的，入迷的</td><td>rapt attention<br>全神贯注</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The children listened with rapt attention as the old sailor spoke.  老水手讲述时，孩子们全神贯注地听着。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 elated</td><td>/ɪˈleɪtəd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>兴高采烈的，得意的</td><td>elated by the news<br>因消息而狂喜</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She was elated when the university finally accepted her application.  大学终于录取她时，她欣喜若狂。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 elevated</td><td>/ˈeləˌveɪtɪd/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>高尚的，崇高的；升高的</td><td>elevated language<br>高雅的语言</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The speech used elevated language that moved the crowd.  这篇演讲用高雅的语言感动了人群。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 enraptured</td><td>/enˈræptʃərd/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>欣喜若狂的，着迷的</td><td>an enraptured audience<br>如痴如醉的观众</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The enraptured audience refused to leave after three encores.  如痴如醉的观众在三次返场后仍不肯走。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 entranced</td><td>/ɪnˈtrænst/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>出神的，入迷的</td><td>entranced by the music<br>被音乐迷住</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The children sat entranced by the puppet show for an hour.  孩子们入迷地看了一小时木偶戏。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 euphoric</td><td>/juˈfɔːrɪk/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>极度愉快的，欣快的</td><td>a euphoric mood<br>欣快的情绪</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The team was euphoric after winning on the final kick.  最后一脚制胜后全队欣喜若狂。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 exhilarated</td><td>/ɪɡˈzɪləreɪtɪd/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>兴奋的，欢欣的</td><td>exhilarated by the climb<br>因攀登而振奋</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They felt exhilarated after skiing down the long open slope.  滑下那条长长的开阔雪道后，他们感到无比振奋。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 intoxicated</td><td>/ɪnˈtɑːksəˌkeɪtɪd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>陶醉的；醉酒的</td><td>intoxicated with success<br>因成功而陶醉</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Intoxicated with early success, the founders ignored every warning.  被早期成功陶醉，创始人们无视了每个警告。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 rapturous</td><td>/ˈræptʃərəs/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>欣喜若狂的，狂热的</td><td>rapturous applause<br>狂热的掌声</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her final aria met rapturous applause from the packed hall.  她最后一段咏叹调引来满场狂热的掌声。</td></tr>
</tbody>
