<tr><td colspan="7">例 I owe my colleagues a great deal for covering my shifts.  同事们替我值班，我欠他们很多。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>reckless</b></td><td>/ˈrekləs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不考虑后果的，大胆鲁莽的</td><td>reckless driving<br>鲁莽驾驶</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His reckless driving on the icy road caused a serious accident.  他在结冰路面上的鲁莽驾驶造成了一场严重事故。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 audacious</td><td>/ɔːˈdeɪʃəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>大胆的，（但往往）愚勇的；令人吃惊的</td><td>an audacious plan<br>大胆的计划</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The general approved an audacious plan to cross the river at night.  将军批准了一个夜间渡河的大胆计划。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 bold</td><td>/ˈboʊld/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>大胆的，勇敢的；醒目的</td><td>a bold move<br>大胆的举动</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It was a bold decision to leave a secure job.  辞去稳定的工作是个大胆的决定。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 brash</td><td>/bræʃ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>愚勇的，鲁莽的</td><td>a brash young executive<br>鲁莽的年轻主管</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His brash remarks offended nearly everyone at the dinner table.  他鲁莽的言论几乎冒犯了餐桌上的每个人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 brassy</td><td>/ˈbræsɪ/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>厚脸皮的，不知羞耻的</td><td>a brassy manner<br>厚脸皮的态度</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her brassy confidence annoyed the older members of the committee.  她那种不知羞耻的自信惹恼了委员会的老成员。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 brazen</td><td>/ˈbreɪzn/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词、动词</td><td>蛮横大胆的，厚颜无耻的；大胆自信地去面对或从事</td><td>a brazen lie<br>无耻的谎言</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She told a brazen lie without even lowering her eyes.  她说了个无耻的谎言，连眼睛都没垂下。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 careless</td><td>/ˈkerləs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>粗心的，疏忽的</td><td>a careless mistake<br>粗心的错误</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 One careless remark ruined months of patient negotiation.  一句不慎的话毁了数月的耐心谈判。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 daredevil</td><td>/ˈderdevl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>大胆鲁莽的（人）</td><td>a daredevil stunt<br>一次鲁莽的特技</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The daredevil rider jumped his motorcycle over six parked cars.  那位鲁莽大胆的骑手驾摩托车跃过六辆停放的汽车。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 rash</td><td>/ræʃ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>草率的，仓促的</td><td>a rash decision<br>草率的决定</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He regretted his rash decision to sell the house so quickly.  他后悔自己如此草率地决定迅速卖掉房子。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 madcap</td><td>/ˈmædkæp/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>莽撞冲动的；莽撞冲动的人</td><td>a madcap adventure<br>一场冒险的疯狂之旅</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Only a madcap would try to cross that river during the flood.  只有热爱冒险的疯子才会在洪水期试图渡过那条河。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 temerarious</del></td><td>/ˌtɛməˈrɛriəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>鲁莽的，胆大妄为的</td><td>a temerarious decision<br>一个鲁莽的决定</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It was temerarious of him to sail alone in that storm.  他在那场风暴中独自出海实在鲁莽。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 careful</td><td>/ˈkerfəl/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>谨慎的，仔细的</td><td>careful consideration<br>慎重考虑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Be careful when you cross this busy street at night.  夜里穿过这条繁忙的街道时要小心。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 cautious</td><td>/ˈkɔːʃəs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>谨慎的，小心的</td><td>a cautious approach<br>谨慎的做法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Investors remained cautious until the election results were announced.  在选举结果公布前投资者一直很谨慎。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 circumspect</td><td>/ˈsɜːrkəmspekt/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>谨慎的，小心的</td><td>remain circumspect in public<br>在公开场合保持谨慎</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Diplomats stay circumspect in public and blunt behind closed doors.  外交官在公开场合谨慎，关起门来则直言不讳。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 guarded</td><td>/ˈɡɑːrdəd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>谨慎的，有保留的</td><td>a guarded response<br>有保留的回应</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The minister gave a guarded welcome to the new proposal.  部长对新提案表示了有保留的欢迎。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 heedful</del></td><td>/ˈhiːdfəl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>留心的，注意的</td><td>heedful of the warning<br>留意警告</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Heedful of the forecast, the climbers turned back early.  留意到天气预报，登山者提早返回了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 prudent</td><td>/ˈpruːdnt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>明智的；小心谨慎的，审慎的</td><td>a prudent investment<br>一笔审慎的投资</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It would be prudent to save part of every paycheck.  每份工资都存下一部分才是明智之举。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 wary</td><td>/ˈweri/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>警惕的，谨慎的</td><td>wary of strangers<br>对陌生人警惕</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Children are taught to be wary of strangers offering free rides.  孩子们被教导要警惕主动提供搭车的陌生人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>recluse</b></td><td>/ˈrekluːs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>隐士</td><td>live as a recluse<br>过隐居生活</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The poet lived as a recluse in a cabin deep in the woods.  这位诗人隐居在林深处的一间小屋里。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 anchorite</td><td>/ˈæŋkəˌraɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>（宗教）隐修者，隐士</td><td>a desert anchorite<br>一位沙漠隐修者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The medieval anchorite spent forty years walled in a tiny cell.  这位中世纪隐修士在一间小室里封闭生活了四十年。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 eremite</del></td><td>/ˈɛrəˌmaɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>（宗教）隐士，苦修者</td><td>a solitary eremite<br>一位独居的苦修隐士</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Legend says an eremite lived in that cave for decades.  传说有位隐士在那个洞穴里住了几十年。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 hermit</td><td>/ˈhɜːrmət/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>隐士，遁世者</td><td>an old hermit<br>一位年迈的隐士</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 After his wife died he became a hermit in the hills.  妻子去世后，他成了山中的隐士。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 isolate</td><td>/ˈaɪsəˌleɪt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>隔离，使孤立</td><td>isolate a patient<br>隔离病人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Doctors isolated the sick passengers as soon as the ship docked.  船一靠岸，医生就隔离了生病的乘客。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 solitary</td><td>/ˈsɑːləˌteri/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>独居的，孤独的</td><td>a solitary life<br>独居的生活</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old writer led a solitary life high in the mountains.  这位老作家在高山上过着独居生活。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 cloistered</td><td>/ˈklɔɪstərd/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>隐居的，与世隔绝的</td><td>a cloistered life<br>隐居的生活</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He led a cloistered life among books, rarely meeting strangers.  他在书堆中过着与世隔绝的生活，很少见陌生人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 hermetic</td><td>/hərˈmetɪk/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>深奥的；密封的</td><td>a hermetic style<br>晦涩的风格</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The poet's hermetic style baffles most readers.  这位诗人晦涩的风格令多数读者困惑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 secluded</td><td>/sɪˈkluːdɪd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>僻静的，隐蔽的</td><td>a secluded cottage<br>一间僻静的小屋</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They honeymooned in a secluded valley far from any road.  他们在远离公路的僻静山谷度蜜月。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 secluse</del></td><td>/sɪˈklus/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>隐居的，与世隔绝的</td><td>a secluse existence<br>隐居的生活</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The poet led a secluse life in a mountain hut.  这位诗人在山间小屋过着隐居生活。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 seclusive</del></td><td>/sɪˈklusɪv/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>喜隐居的，孤僻的</td><td>a seclusive temperament<br>喜好独处的性情</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His seclusive habits puzzled neighbors who never saw him outdoors.  他孤僻的习性让从未见他外出的邻居们困惑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 sequestered</td><td>/sɪˈkwestərd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>隐退的，僻静的</td><td>a sequestered garden<br>一座幽僻的花园</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The monks study in a sequestered wing closed to visitors.  僧侣们在一处不对外开放的幽僻侧厅研习。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 gregarious</td><td>/ɡrɪˈɡeriəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>爱社交的；群居的</td><td>a gregarious personality<br>爱社交的性格</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Unlike his shy brother, he is gregarious and knows everyone in town.  与害羞的弟弟不同，他爱社交，认识镇上所有人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 social</td><td>/ˈsoʊʃəl/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>爱交际的；社会的</td><td>a social animal<br>群居动物</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Humans are social creatures who need regular company and talk.  人是需要经常陪伴与交谈的社会性生物。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>recoil</b></td><td>/rɪˈkɔɪl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>退却，畏缩</td><td>recoil in horror<br>惊恐地退缩</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She recoiled in horror when the lamp revealed the broken door.  灯光照出被砸坏的门时，她惊恐地退缩了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 blench</td><td>/blɛntʃ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>动词</td><td>畏缩，退缩</td><td>blench at the sight<br>见状畏缩</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He did not blench at the sight of the deep wound.  看到那道深伤口他也没有畏缩。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 cringe</td><td>/ˈkrɪndʒ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>卑躬屈膝；畏缩</td><td>cringe before the boss<br>在老板面前卑躬屈膝</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The servant cringed whenever the duke raised his voice.  公爵一提高声音，仆人就畏缩起来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 flinch</td><td>/flɪntʃ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>畏缩，退缩；回避</td><td>flinch from the truth<br>不敢面对真相</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He never flinched from telling his patients the difficult truth.  他从不回避向病人讲述艰难的真相。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 quail</td><td>/kweɪl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>胆怯，畏缩</td><td>quail at the thought<br>一想到就发怵</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Even veteran soldiers quail at the thought of another winter campaign.  连老兵一想到又一场冬季战役也会畏缩。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 shrink</td><td>/ʃrɪŋk/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>（在数量或价值方面）降低，减小</td><td>shrink in value<br>价值缩水</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The value of the old coins began to shrink after the scandal.  丑闻之后，那些旧币的价值开始缩水。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 squinch</del></td><td>/skwɪntʃ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>动词</td><td>（因害怕、不适）皱缩，缩紧</td><td>squinch up her face<br>把脸皱成一团</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The child squinched her eyes shut before the shot.  打针前那孩子把眼睛紧紧闭成一条缝。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 wince</td><td>/wɪns/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>畏缩</td><td>wince at the pain<br>因疼痛而龇牙</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She winced as the nurse cleaned the deep cut on her arm.  护士清洗她手臂上的深口子时，她疼得直畏缩。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 confront</td><td>/kənˈfrʌnt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>直接对抗，直面</td><td>confront the problem<br>直面问题</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Sooner or later you must confront the problem directly.  你早晚必须直面这个问题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 meet</td><td>/miːt/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>合适的</td><td>a meet response<br>合适的回应</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A public apology seemed the only meet response to such a serious blunder.  公开道歉似乎是对如此严重失误唯一合适的回应。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>reconcile</b></td><td>/ˈrekənsaɪl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使和解，协调</td><td>reconcile conflicting views<br>调和相互冲突的观点</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It is hard to reconcile these two versions of the story.  很难把这个故事的两种说法协调一致。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 accommodate</td><td>/əˈkɑːmədeɪt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使适应；容纳；迁就</td><td>accommodate different needs<br>满足不同需求</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The schedule was changed to accommodate everyone's travel plans.  日程被改动以适应每个人的出行计划。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 attune</td><td>/əˈtuːn/</td><td>☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使协调，使和谐</td><td>attune oneself to<br>使自己适应</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Good teachers are attuned to the moods of their students.  好老师能敏锐感知学生的情绪。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 conciliate</td><td>/kənˈsɪlieɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>安抚，平息；调解</td><td>conciliate angry customers<br>安抚愤怒的顾客</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The manager tried to conciliate the angry customers with refunds.  经理试图用退款来安抚愤怒的顾客。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 conform</td><td>/kənˈfɔːrm/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词、不及物动词</td><td>使协调；遵照，遵从</td><td>conform to the standard<br>符合标准</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 All new buildings must conform to strict safety codes.  所有新建筑都必须符合严格的安全规范。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 coordinate</td><td>/koʊˈɔːrdɪneɪt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>及物动词、形容词</td><td>协调，使配合</td><td>coordinate the relief effort<br>协调救援工作</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Two agencies coordinated the rescue effort after the flood.  洪水过后两个机构协调了救援工作。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 harmonize</td><td>/ˈhɑːrməˌnaɪz/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>协调一致；使和谐</td><td>harmonize with tradition<br>与传统相协调</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The new building harmonizes well with the older houses nearby.  这座新建筑与附近的老房子十分协调。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 key</td><td>/ˈkiː/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>关键的；钥匙</td><td>a key factor<br>关键因素</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Trust is the key element in any long working relationship.  信任是任何长期工作关系中的关键要素。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 disharmonize</del></td><td>/dɪsˈhɑrmənaɪz/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使不协调，使失和</td><td>disharmonize a group<br>使团体失和</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Careless remarks can disharmonize an otherwise united team.  不慎的言论会使原本团结的团队失和。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>recondite</b></td><td>/ˈrekəndaɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>深奥的，难解的</td><td>recondite arguments<br>深奥的论证</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The lecture grew so recondite that half the audience quietly left.  讲座变得如此深奥，一半听众悄悄离场。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 abstruse</td><td>/əbˈstruːs/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>难以理解的</td><td>abstruse theories<br>深奥的理论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His lecture was too abstruse for first-year students.  他的讲座对一年级学生来说太晦涩。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 arcane</td><td>/ɑːrˈkeɪn/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>深奥的，难以理解的</td><td>arcane rules<br>晦涩难懂的规则</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The tax code is full of arcane rules nobody understands.  税法充满无人理解的晦涩规定。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 deep</td><td>/ˈdiːp/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>深刻的，深奥的</td><td>a deep analysis<br>深刻的分析</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The essay offers a deep reading of the novel's ending.  这篇文章对小说结尾作了深刻解读。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 enigmatic</td><td>/ˌenɪɡˈmætɪk/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>神秘的，难以理解的</td><td>an enigmatic smile<br>神秘的微笑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her enigmatic answer left the reporters guessing for several days.  她神秘的回答让记者们猜了好几天。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 esoteric</td><td>/ˌesəˈterɪk/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>深奥难懂的</td><td>esoteric knowledge<br>深奥的知识</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She writes about esoteric topics in medieval music.  她写中世纪音乐中深奥的题目。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 hermetic</td><td>/hərˈmetɪk/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>深奥的；密封的</td><td>a hermetic style<br>晦涩的风格</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The poet's hermetic style baffles most readers.  这位诗人晦涩的风格令多数读者困惑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 occult</td><td>/əˈkʌlt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>难以理解的</td><td>occult knowledge<br>玄奥的知识</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The manuscript is filled with occult knowledge that scholars still cannot decipher.  这份手稿满是学者至今无法解读的玄奥知识。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 profound</td><td>/proʊˈfaʊnd/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>深刻的，深远的</td><td>a profound influence<br>深远的影响</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her death had a profound effect on the whole family.  她的死对整个家庭产生了深远的影响。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 facile</td><td>/ˈfæsl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>容易做到的；肤浅轻率的</td><td>a facile explanation<br>肤浅的解释</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The report offers a facile explanation for a very complex problem.  这份报告对一个非常复杂的问题给出了肤浅的解释。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 shallow</td><td>/ˈʃæloʊ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>浅薄的，肤浅的；浅的</td><td>shallow water<br>浅水</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His arguments are shallow and easy to refute.  他的论点浅薄，很容易反驳。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 superficial</td><td>/ˌsuːpərˈfɪʃl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>表面的，肤浅的</td><td>a superficial understanding<br>肤浅的理解</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She has only a superficial knowledge of the subject.  她对这门学科只有肤浅的了解。</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

## Unit 10

<table>
<thead><tr>
<th>单词</th>
<th>音标</th>
<th>常用</th>
<th>口语</th>
<th>词性</th>
<th>中文释义</th>
<th>常用短语</th>
</tr></thead>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>reconnoiter</b></td><td>/ˌriːkəˈnɔɪtər/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>侦察，勘查</td><td>reconnoiter enemy positions<br>侦察敌方阵地</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Two scouts went ahead at dawn to reconnoiter the enemy positions.  两名侦察兵黎明先行去侦察敌方阵地。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 probe</td><td>/proʊb/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>深入调查</td><td>probe deeply into the scandal<br>深入调查这桩丑闻</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Investigators continued to probe deeply into the company's missing funds.  调查人员继续深入调查公司资金失踪的问题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 scout</td><td>/ˈskaʊt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>嘲笑，轻蔑地拒绝；侦察</td><td>scout the suggestion<br>嘲笑该建议</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old captain scouted every warning the young sailors offered.  老船长嘲笑年轻水手提出的每句警告。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 survey</td><td>/sərˈveɪ/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>调查；勘测；概览</td><td>conduct a survey<br>开展一项调查</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 We surveyed every household about their water use last summer.  去年夏天我们调查了每户人家的用水情况。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>reconstitute</b></td><td>/ˌriːˈkɑːnstətuːt/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>重建，（尤其是通过加水）使复原</td><td>reconstitute dried milk powder<br>把奶粉加水复原</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Simply add warm water to reconstitute the dried milk powder.  只需加温水就能把奶粉复原。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 reconstruct</td><td>/ˌriːkənˈstrʌkt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>重建，重构；再现</td><td>reconstruct the crime scene<br>重现犯罪现场</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Engineers reconstructed the bridge using the original nineteenth-century plans.  工程师们依照十九世纪的原始图纸重建了这座桥。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 reorder</td><td>/riˈɔːrdər/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>重新排列；再订购</td><td>reorder the priorities<br>重新排定优先次序</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The new editor reordered the chapters to improve the narrative flow.  新编辑重排了章节顺序以改善叙事节奏。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 reorganize</td><td>/riˈɔːrɡəˌnaɪz/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>重组，改组</td><td>reorganize the department<br>改组该部门</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Management reorganized the sales team after two disappointing quarters.  两个季度业绩不佳后，管理层重组了销售团队。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 reshuffle</td><td>/riˈʃʌfəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>改组，重新调整</td><td>a cabinet reshuffle<br>一次内阁改组</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The prime minister reshuffled his cabinet to quiet the critics.  首相改组内阁以平息批评。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 retool</td><td>/riˈtuːl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>动词</td><td>重新装备；改造，调整</td><td>retool the factory<br>为工厂更换设备</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The company must retool its whole strategy for a digital market.  公司必须为数字市场彻底调整整个战略。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 annihilate</td><td>/əˈnaɪəˌleɪt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>彻底摧毁，歼灭</td><td>annihilate the enemy<br>歼灭敌人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A single flood could annihilate the entire harvest of the valley.  一场洪水就能彻底毁掉整个山谷的收成。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 decimate</td><td>/ˈdesəˌmeɪt/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>大量毁灭，大批杀死</td><td>decimate the population<br>使人口大量减少</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The epidemic decimated herds of cattle across three provinces.  这场疫病使三个省的牛群大量死亡。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 demolish</td><td>/dɪˈmɑːlɪʃ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>拆除，摧毁；驳倒</td><td>demolish an old building<br>拆除旧楼</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The city decided to demolish the unsafe old building last spring.  去年春天市政府决定拆除这栋危旧建筑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 devastate</td><td>/ˈdevəˌsteɪt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>毁灭，破坏；使震惊</td><td>devastate the coastal town<br>摧毁那座海滨小镇</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The earthquake devastated neighborhoods that had stood for a century.  地震摧毁了矗立了一个世纪的街区。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 level</td><td>/ˈlevəl/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>水平的，平的</td><td>on level ground<br>在平地上</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Pitch the tent on level ground away from the stream.  把帐篷搭在远离溪水的平地上。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 nuke</td><td>/ˈnuːk/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>用核武器攻击；彻底摧毁</td><td>nuke a city<br>对一座城市发动核打击</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Generals debated whether to nuke the island rather than invade it.  将军们争论是否用核武器打击该岛而非登陆进攻。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 pulverize</td><td>/ˈpʌlvəraɪz/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>将…研磨成粉；将…彻底摧毁</td><td>pulverize the rock<br>把岩石磨成粉</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Workers used heavy hammers to pulverize the rock into fine powder.  工人用重锤把岩石研磨成细粉。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 ruin</td><td>/ˈruːɪn/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>毁坏，毁灭；废墟</td><td>ruin the harvest<br>毁掉收成</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Weeks of drought ruined the harvest and emptied the village granary.  数周干旱毁掉收成，村里粮仓也空了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 smash</td><td>/ˈsmæʃ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>轰动的成功；打碎</td><td>a box-office smash<br>票房大热之作</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The low-budget comedy became a box-office smash overnight.  这部低成本喜剧一夜之间成为票房大热。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 vaporize</td><td>/ˈveɪpəraɪz/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>彻底消灭</td><td>vaporize the target<br>把目标彻底毁灭</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A single blast vaporized the abandoned tower on the distant hill.  一次爆炸就把远处山上那座废弃塔楼彻底摧毁。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 wreck</td><td>/ˈrek/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>残骸；失事；破坏</td><td>the wreck of a ship<br>一艘船的残骸</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Divers photographed the wreck lying deep on the sandy bottom.  潜水员拍摄了深卧沙底的残骸。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>repentant</b></td><td>/rɪˈpentənt/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>悔过的</td><td>sound repentant<br>听来悔过</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The thief looked genuinely repentant as he returned the money to its owner.  那名小偷把钱还给主人时显得真心悔过。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 apologetic</td><td>/əˌpɑːləˈdʒetɪk/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>道歉的，愧疚的</td><td>an apologetic smile<br>带着歉意的微笑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She sent an apologetic note explaining why she had missed dinner.  她送来一张致歉的便条，解释为何缺席晚餐。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 compunctious</del></td><td>/kəmˈpʌŋkʃəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>内疚的，懊悔的</td><td>a compunctious glance<br>内疚的一瞥</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He gave her a compunctious glance and quietly returned the money.  他内疚地看了她一眼，悄悄把钱还了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 contrite</td><td>/kənˈtraɪt/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>（因为有罪孽或过错而感到）后悔悲痛的</td><td>a contrite apology<br>悔痛的道歉</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He looked genuinely contrite as he apologized for breaking the window.  他为打破窗户道歉时显得真心悔痛。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 penitent</td><td>/ˈpenɪtənt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>悔过的</td><td>a penitent sinner<br>悔过的罪人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The penitent thief returned the money and apologized to the shopkeeper.  那个悔过的小偷退还了钱，并向店主道歉。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 regretful</td><td>/rɪˈɡretfəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>后悔的，遗憾的</td><td>a regretful glance back<br>懊悔的回望</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She sounded regretful when she declined the invitation.  她拒绝邀请时语气中带着遗憾。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 remorseful</td><td>/rɪˈmɔːrsfəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>悔恨的，自责的</td><td>look deeply remorseful<br>显得深深悔恨</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The young thief seemed genuinely remorseful in front of the judge.  这个年轻小偷在法官面前显得真心悔恨。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 rueful</td><td>/ˈruːfəl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>悔恨的，懊悔的</td><td>a rueful smile<br>懊悔的苦笑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He admitted his error with a rueful smile and a shrug.  他带着懊悔的苦笑和一个耸肩承认了错误。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 sorry</td><td>/ˈsɑːri/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>懊悔的，抱歉的</td><td>genuinely sorry for it<br>真心为此懊悔</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He seemed genuinely sorry for the trouble he caused.  他似乎真心为自己造成的麻烦懊悔。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 impenitent</del></td><td>/ɪmˈpenɪtənt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不悔悟的，死不认错的</td><td>an impenitent offender<br>毫无悔意的罪犯</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The thief remained impenitent even after hearing the victims speak.  即便听了受害者的陈述，窃贼仍毫无悔意。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 remorseless</td><td>/rɪˈmɔːrsləs/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>毫无悔意的，冷酷无情的</td><td>remorseless cruelty<br>冷酷无情的残忍</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The killer remained remorseless throughout the long public trial.  凶手在漫长的公开审判中始终毫无悔意。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unapologetic</td><td>/ˌʌnəpɑːləˈdʒetɪk/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不认错的，毫无悔意的</td><td>unapologetic about the decision<br>对该决定毫无悔意</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She remained unapologetic about the harsh review.  她对那篇苛评始终毫无悔意。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unrepentant</td><td>/ˌʌnrɪˈpentənt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不悔悟的，毫无悔意的</td><td>remain unrepentant<br>仍毫无悔意</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Even after the verdict he remained completely unrepentant about the fraud.  即使判决之后，他对这场欺诈仍毫无悔意。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>rectitude</b></td><td>/ˈrektɪtuːd/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>正直；品行端正</td><td>moral rectitude<br>道德上的正直</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old judge was admired for his moral rectitude and quiet courage.  这位老法官因道德上的正直和沉静的勇气而受人敬仰。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 honesty</td><td>/ˈɑːnəsti/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>诚实，正直</td><td>honesty in business<br>生意上的诚信</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her honesty about the mistake earned everyone's respect.  她对错误的坦诚赢得了所有人的尊重。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 integrity</td><td>/ɪnˈteɡrəti/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>正直；完整性</td><td>a man of integrity<br>正直的人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Everyone respected the judge as a man of unshakable integrity and courage.  所有人都尊敬这位法官，视他为正直不屈且勇气可嘉的人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 probity</td><td>/ˈproʊbəti/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>正直</td><td>unquestioned probity<br>毋庸置疑的正直</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The judge was known for probity and unfailing patience.  这位法官以正直和永不失去的耐心闻名。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 righteousness</td><td>/ˈraɪtʃəsnəs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>正直，正义</td><td>a sense of righteousness<br>正义感</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He defended the poor with a quiet righteousness that impressed everyone.  他以一种沉静的正义感为穷人辩护，令所有人钦佩。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 scrupulousness</del></td><td>/ˈskruːpjələsnəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>严谨，一丝不苟</td><td>scrupulousness in accounting<br>记账的严谨</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her scrupulousness about sources impressed the editors greatly.  她对资料来源的严谨令编辑们印象深刻。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 uprightness</td><td>/ˈʌpraɪtnəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>正直，诚实</td><td>moral uprightness<br>道德上的正直</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His moral uprightness earned respect even from his opponents.  他道德上的正直连对手都尊敬。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 virtue</td><td>/ˈvɜːrtʃu/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>美德，德行；优点</td><td>a life of virtue<br>有德行的生活</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Patience is a virtue that few travelers keep at airports.  耐心是少有旅客能在机场保持的美德。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 virtuousness</del></td><td>/ˈvɜrtʃuəsnəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>德行，正直</td><td>quiet virtuousness<br>低调的德行</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her virtuousness impressed even those who disagreed with her politics.  她的德行连政见不同的人也为之动容。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 badness</td><td>/ˈbædnəs/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>恶劣，邪恶</td><td>the badness of his conduct<br>他行为的恶劣</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The novel explores the badness lurking in ordinary people.  这部小说探讨潜藏在普通人身上的恶。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 evil</td><td>/ˈiːvəl/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词、形容词</td><td>邪恶；邪恶的</td><td>the lesser evil<br>较小的祸害</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The novel examines how ordinary people come to commit evil.  这部小说探讨普通人如何走向作恶。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 immorality</td><td>/ˌɪməˈrælɪti/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>不道德，邪恶</td><td>public immorality<br>公开的不道德行为</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Preachers denounced the immorality they saw in the new theaters.  传教士谴责他们在新剧院里看到的不道德风气。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 iniquity</td><td>/ɪˈnɪkwəti/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>邪恶，不公正</td><td>a den of iniquity<br>罪恶的窝点</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The novel exposes the iniquity of a legal system that protects only the rich.  这部小说揭露了只保护富人的司法制度的不公。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 sin</td><td>/ˈsɪn/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词、不及物动词</td><td>罪过，过失</td><td>confess one's sins<br>承认自己的罪过</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 In her view, wasting food is a serious sin.  在她看来，浪费食物是严重的过错。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 villainy</td><td>/ˈvɪləni/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>恶行，邪恶</td><td>an act of villainy<br>一桩恶行</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The play delights audiences with the villainy of its scheming duke.  剧中那位阴谋公爵的恶行让观众看得过瘾。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 wickedness</td><td>/ˈwɪkədnəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>邪恶，恶劣</td><td>human wickedness<br>人性的邪恶</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The preacher warned his listeners against greed and wickedness.  牧师警告听众远离贪婪与邪恶。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>recumbent</b></td><td>/rɪˈkʌmbənt/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>躺着的</td><td>a recumbent figure<br>一个卧姿人像</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A recumbent stone figure guards the entrance to the ancient tomb.  一尊卧姿石像守着这座古墓的入口。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 decumbent</td><td>/dɪˈkʌmbənt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>（植物）平卧的；躺着的</td><td>decumbent stems<br>平卧的茎</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 This herb has decumbent stems that spread across the sand.  这种草本植物的茎平卧着铺展在沙地上。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 procumbent</del></td><td>/proʊˈkʌmbənt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>俯卧的；（植物）匍匐的</td><td>a procumbent shrub<br>一种匍匐生长的灌木</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Botanists noted the procumbent growth of the coastal plant.  植物学家记录了这种海岸植物匍匐的生长方式。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 prone</td><td>/ˈproʊn/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>倾向于…的，易于…的</td><td>prone to error<br>易出错的</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Handwritten records are prone to error and hard to search.  手写记录容易出错且难以检索。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 prostrate</td><td>/ˈprɑːstreɪt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词、及物动词</td><td>平躺的，俯卧的；使极度虚弱</td><td>lie prostrate on the ground<br>平躺在地上</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The exhausted runner lay prostrate on the ground after crossing the line.  那名精疲力竭的跑者冲线后平躺在地上。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 reclining</td><td>/rɪˈklaɪnɪŋ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>躺着的，后倾的</td><td>a reclining chair<br>一把可后倾的躺椅</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He read the newspaper in a reclining position by the window.  他在窗边半躺着看报纸。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 supine</td><td>/ˈsuːpaɪn/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>仰卧的；消极的，漠不关心的</td><td>supine acceptance of injustice<br>对不公的消极接受</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The legislature's supine acceptance of injustice angered many ordinary citizens.  立法机关对不公的消极接受激怒了许多普通市民。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 erect</td><td>/ɪˈrekt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词、及物动词</td><td>直立的，垂直的</td><td>stand erect<br>笔直地站着</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old soldier still stood perfectly erect.  这位老兵仍站得笔直。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 upright</td><td>/ˈʌpraɪt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>正直的，诚实的；竖直的</td><td>an upright citizen<br>一位正直的公民</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Neighbors described him as an upright and reliable man.  邻居们说他是个正直可靠的人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>recuperate</b></td><td>/rɪˈkuːpəreɪt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>动词</td><td>康复，恢复（健康或体力）</td><td>recuperate from an illness<br>病后康复</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He spent two weeks at the coast recuperating from surgery.  他在海边待了两周，从手术中康复。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 convalesce</td><td>/ˌkɑːnvəˈles/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>渐渐康复，渐愈</td><td>convalesce at home<br>在家中养病康复</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She spent three quiet weeks convalescing at home after the surgery.  手术后她在家静养康复了三周。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 heal</td><td>/ˈhiːl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>治愈，痊愈</td><td>heal a wound<br>治愈伤口</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The deep cut took nearly a month to heal completely.  那道深伤口花了近一个月才完全愈合。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 rally</td><td>/ˈræli/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>恢复，重振；集合；集会</td><td>rally after surgery<br>手术后恢复过来</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The patient rallied surprisingly quickly after a week of treatment.  经过一周治疗，病人恢复得出人意料地快。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 recoup</td><td>/rɪˈkuːp/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>动词</td><td>恢复，复原；补偿</td><td>recoup his strength<br>恢复体力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 After the long march the soldiers rested to recoup their strength.  长途行军后，士兵们休息以恢复体力。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 recover</td><td>/rɪˈkʌvər/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>康复；恢复，找回</td><td>recover from illness<br>病后康复</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It took him two months to recover from the serious operation.  他花了两个月才从这场大手术中康复。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 rehabilitate</td><td>/ˌriːhəˈbɪlɪteɪt/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使复原，使康复</td><td>rehabilitate former inmates<br>帮助刑满释放者重返社会</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The program aims to rehabilitate former inmates and find them jobs.  该项目旨在帮刑满释放者重返社会并找到工作。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 deteriorate</td><td>/dɪˈtɪriəreɪt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>恶化，变坏</td><td>deteriorate rapidly<br>迅速恶化</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her health deteriorated sharply during the cold winter.  在寒冷的冬天里她的健康急剧恶化。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 flag</td><td>/flæɡ/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>变得衰弱</td><td>his energy began to flag<br>他的精力开始衰减</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His energy began to flag after the twentieth mile.  跑到第二十英里后他的精力开始衰减。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 wane</td><td>/weɪn/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>不及物动词、名词</td><td>减少，衰退</td><td>wax and wane<br>盈亏消长，兴衰</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Public interest in the scandal waned within a month.  公众对这桩丑闻的兴趣一个月内就减退了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 weaken</td><td>/ˈwiːkən/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>削弱，使变弱，变衰弱</td><td>weaken the structure<br>使结构变弱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Repeated flooding weakened the foundations of the stone bridge.  反复的洪水削弱了石桥的地基。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>redolent</b></td><td>/ˈredələnt/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>芳香的；令人想起…的</td><td>redolent of spices<br>散发香料气味</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old room was redolent of pipe smoke and leather.  那间旧屋弥漫着烟斗烟味和皮革气息。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 ambrosial</td><td>/æmˈbroʊziəl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>特别美味的；芬芳的</td><td>an ambrosial dessert<br>美味无比的甜点</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The soup had an ambrosial aroma that filled the whole house.  这汤散发的美妙香气弥漫了整座房子。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 aromatic</td><td>/ˌerəˈmætɪk/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>芳香的</td><td>aromatic herbs<br>芳香的草药</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The kitchen was full of aromatic spices from the market.  厨房里满是从市场买来的芳香香料。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fragrant</td><td>/ˈfreɪɡrənt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>芳香的，香的</td><td>fragrant flowers<br>芳香的花朵</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The kitchen was fragrant with fresh bread and cinnamon.  厨房里弥漫着新鲜面包和肉桂的香气。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 perfumed</td><td>/pərˈfjuːmd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>有香味的，洒了香水的</td><td>perfumed soap<br>香皂</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She wrote the letter on thin perfumed paper.  她用薄薄的香纸写了那封信。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 savory</td><td>/ˈseɪvəri/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>美味的，可口的</td><td>a savory dish<br>一道可口的菜</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She served a savory pie made with mushrooms and herbs.  她端上一道用蘑菇和香草做的美味派。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 scented</td><td>/ˈsentɪd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>有香味的，加香的</td><td>scented candles<br>香味蜡烛</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She wrote all her letters on pale scented paper.  她所有的信都写在淡色的香味纸上。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 sweet</td><td>/ˈswiːt/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>甜的；温和的，亲切的</td><td>in a sweet tone<br>以温和的语气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She spoke in a sweet voice that calmed the frightened child.  她用温柔的声音说话，安抚了害怕的孩子。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unscented</td><td>/ʌnˈsɛntɪd/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>无香味的，不加香料的</td><td>unscented soap<br>无香味的香皂</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She buys unscented lotion because perfumes irritate her skin.  她买无香味的乳液，因为香精会刺激她的皮肤。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>redoubtable</b></td><td>/rɪˈdaʊtəbl/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>杰出的，值得尊敬的；可怕的</td><td>a redoubtable rival<br>令人敬畏的对手</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She faced a redoubtable rival in the final round of the tournament.  她在锦标赛决赛轮中遇上一位令人敬畏的对手。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 bright</td><td>/ˈbraɪt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>快活的，开朗的；明亮的</td><td>a bright smile<br>开朗的笑容</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She greeted every customer with a bright and genuine smile.  她用开朗真诚的笑容迎接每位顾客。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 distinguished</td><td>/dɪˈstɪŋɡwɪʃt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>卓越的，杰出的；高贵的</td><td>a distinguished scholar<br>杰出的学者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The prize went to a distinguished historian of medieval law.  奖项授予一位杰出的中世纪法律史学家。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 illustrious</td><td>/ɪˈlʌstriəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>著名的，杰出的</td><td>an illustrious career<br>辉煌杰出的生涯</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She retired after an illustrious career in medical research.  在医学研究领域度过杰出的生涯后，她退休了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 luminous</td><td>/ˈluːmɪnəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>发光的，明亮的</td><td>a luminous dial<br>夜光表盘</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The luminous dial of his watch glowed faintly in the dark.  他手表的夜光表盘在黑暗中微微发亮。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 notable</td><td>/ˈnoʊtəbəl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>显著的；值得注意的</td><td>a notable achievement<br>显著的成就</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 There has been a notable drop in traffic accidents this year.  今年交通事故有显著下降。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 noteworthy</td><td>/ˈnoʊˌtwɜːrði/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>值得注意的，显著的</td><td>a noteworthy achievement<br>一项值得注意的成就</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The most noteworthy feature of the report is its honesty.  这份报告最值得注意的地方是它的诚实。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 outstanding</td><td>/ˌaʊtˈstændɪŋ/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>杰出的，突出的</td><td>an outstanding performance<br>出色的表现</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She gave an outstanding performance in her very first concert.  她在首场音乐会上表现出色。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 preeminent</td><td>/priˈemɪnənt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>优秀的，重要的</td><td>the preeminent scholar<br>最杰出的学者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He is the preeminent authority on medieval trade routes in Europe.  他是研究欧洲中世纪贸易路线最杰出的权威。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 prestigious</td><td>/preˈstɪdʒəs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>有声望的，受尊敬的</td><td>a prestigious award<br>有声望的奖项</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He turned down a prestigious post to stay near his family.  他为了留在家人身边拒绝了一个有声望的职位。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 direful</del></td><td>/ˈdaɪrfəl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>可怕的，凄惨的</td><td>direful warnings<br>可怕的警告</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old prophet issued direful warnings about the coming winter.  老先知对将至的冬天发出可怕的警告。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dreadful</td><td>/ˈdredfəl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>可怕的，糟透的</td><td>a dreadful mistake<br>可怕的错误</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She had a dreadful headache and went home early.  她头疼得厉害，很早就回家了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fearsome</td><td>/ˈfɪrsəm/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>可怕的，令人畏惧的</td><td>a fearsome reputation<br>令人畏惧的名声</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The boxer had a fearsome reputation long before this fight.  这名拳手在此战之前早已令人畏惧。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 formidable</td><td>/ˈfɔːrmɪdəbl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>难以对付的，令人敬畏的</td><td>a formidable opponent<br>难以对付的对手</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She faced a formidable opponent in the final round.  她在最后一轮遇到了难以对付的对手。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 frightening</td><td>/ˈfraɪtənɪŋ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>令人害怕的</td><td>a frightening noise<br>令人害怕的声响</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A frightening crack came from somewhere under the ice.  冰层下某处传来令人害怕的开裂声。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 ghastly</td><td>/ˈɡæstli/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>可怕的；惨白的</td><td>a ghastly discovery<br>可怕的发现</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He turned a ghastly white when he heard the news.  他听到消息时脸色惨白。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 horrifying</td><td>/ˈhɔːrəˌfaɪɪŋ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>令人震惊恐惧的</td><td>horrifying footage<br>令人震惊的画面</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Rescuers described horrifying scenes inside the collapsed apartment block.  救援人员描述了倒塌公寓楼内令人震惊的景象。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 intimidating</td><td>/ɪnˈtɪmɪdeɪtɪŋ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>令人畏惧的，恐吓的</td><td>an intimidating silence<br>令人发怵的沉默</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The examiner's intimidating stare made the candidate forget everything.  主考官令人发怵的注视让考生忘了一切。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 scary</td><td>/ˈskeri/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>可怕的，吓人的</td><td>a scary movie<br>恐怖电影</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The rope bridge looked scary but felt perfectly solid underfoot.  那座绳桥看着吓人，踩上去却很稳。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 terrifying</td><td>/ˈterəˌfaɪɪŋ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>令人恐惧的</td><td>a terrifying experience<br>令人恐惧的经历</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Landing in that storm was the most terrifying moment yet.  在那场风暴中降落是至今最恐怖的时刻。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 average</td><td>/ˈævərɪdʒ/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>平庸的，一般的；平均数</td><td>an average student<br>成绩平平的学生</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His work is average but it always arrives on time.  他的活儿一般，但总能按时交。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 inferior</td><td>/ɪnˈfɪriər/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>低等的，次等的</td><td>inferior in quality<br>质量较差</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Customers complained that the new fabric was clearly inferior.  顾客抱怨新面料明显质量较差。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 mediocre</td><td>/ˌmiːdiˈoʊkər/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>平庸的；质量中等偏下的</td><td>a mediocre performance<br>平庸的表演</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The orchestra's mediocre performance disappointed listeners who had waited months for the concert.  乐团平庸的表演让等了几个月的听众很失望。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 calming</td><td>/ˈkɑːmɪŋ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>令人平静的，使人安心的</td><td>a calming voice<br>令人平静的声音</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The nurse spoke in a calming tone until the child relaxed.  护士用令人安心的语调说话，直到孩子放松下来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 comforting</td><td>/ˈkʌmfərtɪŋ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>令人安慰的，宽慰的</td><td>a comforting thought<br>一个令人安慰的想法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It was comforting to know that help was only minutes away.  知道救援只有几分钟就到，这很让人安心。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 consoling</td><td>/kənˈsoʊlɪŋ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>安慰的，慰藉的</td><td>a consoling embrace<br>一个安慰的拥抱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He offered a few consoling words before leaving the hospital room.  他在离开病房前说了几句安慰的话。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 lulling</td><td>/ˈlʌlɪŋ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>使人昏昏欲睡的，安抚的</td><td>the lulling rhythm of waves<br>海浪令人欲睡的节奏</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The lulling hum of the train soon put everyone to sleep.  火车令人欲睡的嗡嗡声很快让所有人都睡着了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 pacifying</td><td>/ˈpæsɪˌfaɪɪŋ/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>抚慰的，使平静的</td><td>a pacifying gesture<br>一个安抚的手势</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her pacifying manner defused the argument within minutes.  她安抚的态度几分钟内就化解了争执。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 quieting</td><td>/ˈkwaɪətɪŋ/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>使安静的，镇定的</td><td>a quieting effect<br>使人安静的作用</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Soft lighting had a quieting effect on the restless ward.  柔和的灯光让不安的病房安静了下来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 reassuring</td><td>/ˌriːəˈʃʊrɪŋ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>令人安心的，使人放心的</td><td>a reassuring smile<br>一个让人放心的微笑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The pilot's reassuring announcement calmed the nervous passengers.  机长令人安心的广播让紧张的乘客平静下来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 relaxing</td><td>/rɪˈlæksɪŋ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>令人放松的，轻松的</td><td>a relaxing bath<br>一次放松的泡澡</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Nothing is more relaxing than an afternoon beside a quiet lake.  没有什么比在静湖边度过一个下午更放松了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 soothing</td><td>/ˈsuːðɪŋ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>抚慰的，缓解疼痛的</td><td>a soothing ointment<br>一种止痛药膏</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She played soothing music to help the baby fall asleep.  她放着舒缓的音乐帮助婴儿入睡。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>redundant</b></td><td>/rɪˈdʌndənt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>多余的，冗余的</td><td>redundant information<br>多余的信息</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The editor cut several paragraphs of redundant information from the report.  编辑从报告中删去了几段多余的信息。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 excess</td><td>/ˈekˌses/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词、形容词</td><td>过量，过剩；额外的</td><td>an excess of caution<br>过度的谨慎</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Trim any excess fabric before you sew the final seam.  在缝最后一道线前先剪掉多余的布料。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 extra</td><td>/ˈekstrə/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>额外的，多余的</td><td>an extra copy<br>多出的一份副本</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 We packed extra blankets in case the night turned cold.  我们多带了几条毯子，以防夜里变冷。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 supererogatory</del></td><td>/ˌsupərɪˈrɑɡəˌtɔri/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>分外的，多余的</td><td>a supererogatory gesture<br>一个分外之举</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Sending flowers as well seemed a supererogatory act of kindness.  再送花显得是分外的好意了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 superfluous</td><td>/suːˈpɜːrfluəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>多余的，过剩的</td><td>superfluous detail<br>多余的细节</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The editor cut every superfluous detail from the opening chapter.  编辑删掉了开篇一章中所有多余的细节。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 supernumerary</td><td>/ˌsupərˈnuməˌrɛri/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>多余的；临时人员，配角</td><td>a supernumerary clerk<br>一名编外职员</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Two supernumerary actors filled the crowd scene without speaking.  两名临时演员在群众场面中充当配角，没有台词。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 surplus</td><td>/ˈsɜːrpləs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词、形容词</td><td>过剩，剩余</td><td>a grain surplus<br>粮食过剩</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The country exported its grain surplus to neighboring markets.  该国把过剩的粮食出口到邻国市场。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 deficient</td><td>/dɪˈfɪʃənt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不足的，缺乏的</td><td>deficient in vitamins<br>缺乏维生素</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The diet was deficient in iron and essential vitamins.  这种饮食缺乏铁和必需的维生素。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 inadequate</td><td>/ɪnˈædəkwət/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不充足的，不胜任的</td><td>inadequate funding<br>资金不足</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The lighting in the room is completely inadequate.  房间的照明完全不够。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 insufficient</td><td>/ˌɪnsəˈfɪʃənt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不足的，不够的</td><td>insufficient evidence<br>证据不足</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The evidence was insufficient to convict him of the crime.  证据不足，无法判定他有罪。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 meager</td><td>/ˈmiːɡər/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>贫乏的</td><td>a meager income<br>微薄的收入</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They raised four children on a meager income from a small roadside shop.  他们靠路边小店微薄的收入养育了四个孩子。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 niggardly</td><td>/ˈnɪɡərdli/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>吝啬的，少得可怜的</td><td>a niggardly raise<br>少得可怜的加薪</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The company offered a niggardly raise after record profits.  在创纪录盈利后公司给了少得可怜的加薪。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 scant</td><td>/skænt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不足的，缺乏的</td><td>scant attention<br>很少的关注</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The report paid scant attention to the concerns of local residents.  报告对当地居民的关切几乎没有给予关注。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 scarce</td><td>/ˈskers/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>稀缺的，不足的</td><td>in scarce supply<br>供应短缺</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Fresh water became scarce during the drought.  旱灾期间淡水变得稀缺。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 short</td><td>/ˈʃɔːrt/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>短缺的，不足的</td><td>short of money<br>缺钱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 We are short of money until the end of the month.  到月底之前我们都缺钱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 sparse</td><td>/spɑːrs/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>稀疏的，稀少的</td><td>sparse vegetation<br>稀疏的植被</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Vegetation grows sparse as the trail climbs above the tree line.  小径爬上林线以上后，植被变得稀疏。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>reel</b></td><td>/riːl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>感到眩晕</td><td>reel from the blow<br>被打得头昏眼花</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The boxer reeled from the blow and clutched at the ropes.  拳手被那一击打得头昏眼花，抓住了围绳。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 spin</td><td>/ˈspɪn/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>旋转；纺纱；快速转动</td><td>spin like a top<br>像陀螺一样旋转</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Watch the wooden top spin steadily on the smooth kitchen table.  看那木陀螺在光滑的餐桌上平稳旋转。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 swirl</td><td>/ˈswɜːrl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>打旋，涡动；漩涡</td><td>swirl the wine<br>摇晃酒杯让酒打旋</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Dead leaves swirled across the empty square in the wind.  枯叶在风中打着旋掠过空荡的广场。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 turn</td><td>/ˈtɜːrn/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>转动，旋转；转向</td><td>turn on its axis<br>绕轴旋转</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The heavy wheel turned slowly as the miller released the water.  磨坊主放水时，那沉重的轮子慢慢转动起来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 whirl</td><td>/ˈwɜːrl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>急转，飞旋；旋转</td><td>whirl around<br>猛地转过身</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Snowflakes whirled madly in the beam of the streetlight.  雪花在路灯光柱中疯狂飞旋。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 calm</td><td>/ˈkɑːm/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>形容词、动词、名词</td><td>平静的；使镇静</td><td>calm down quickly<br>很快平静下来</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She spoke in a calm voice until the children settled.  她用平静的声音说话，直到孩子们安静下来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
